of independent identical distributed random  $d \times d$  matrices, each satisfying with high probability  $\frac{7}{8}\mathbf{n}^{(k)} \prec \mathbf{m}^{-1} \prec \frac{8}{7}\mathbf{n}^{(k)}$ . let  $x$  be a geometric distributed random variable with  $\mathbb{p}[x \geq k] = 2^{-k}$ .

for any vector  $b \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and any initial vector  $y^{(0)}$ , define  $y^{(i+1)} = 2(y^{(i)} - \frac{1}{2}y^{(0)} + \mathbf{n}^{(i+1)}(b - \mathbf{m}^{(i+1)}y^{(i)}))$  for all  $i \geq 0$ . then  $\mathbb{e}[y^{(x)}] = \mathbf{m}^{-1}b$ , and with high probability we have

$$
\left\| y ^ {(x)} - y ^ {(0)} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m}} \leq 3 0 \| b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} + 3 0 \epsilon \| b \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}}. \tag {8.1}
$$

proof. we first show that  $\mathbb{e}[y^{(x)}]$  exists. to show that, we first bound  $\| y^{(i)} - y^{(0)}\|_{\mathbf{m}}$  for all  $i$ . note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} y ^ {(i)} - y ^ {(0)} = 2 y ^ {(i - 1)} - 2 y ^ {(0)} + 2 \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(i - 1)}) \\ = 2 (\mathbf {i} - \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) (y ^ {(i - 1)} - y ^ {(0)}) + 2 \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(0)}). \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we have

$$
\| y ^ {(i)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \leq 2 \| \mathbf {i} - \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \| y ^ {(i - 1)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} + 2 \| \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(0)}) \| _ {\mathbf {m}}. \tag {8.2}
$$

note that

$$
(\frac {7}{8}) ^ {2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1} \preceq \frac {7}{8} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1} \preceq \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \preceq \frac {8}{7} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1} \preceq (\frac {8}{7}) ^ {2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1}.
$$

hence, we have

$$
- 0. 3 1 \cdot \mathbf {i} \preceq (1 - (\frac {8}{7}) ^ {2}) \mathbf {i} \preceq \mathbf {i} - (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \preceq (1 - (\frac {7}{8}) ^ {2}) \mathbf {i} \preceq 0. 2 4 \cdot \mathbf {i}.
$$

using  $\frac{7}{8}\mathbf{m} \preceq \mathbf{m}^{(i)} \preceq \frac{8}{7}\mathbf{m}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {i} - \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m}} = \left\| \mathbf {i} - \mathbf {m} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {i} - \mathbf {m} ^ {1 / 2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \\ \leq \| \mathbf {m} ^ {1 / 2} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \| \mathbf {i} - (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \| (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \\ \leq \sqrt {\frac {7}{8}} \cdot 0. 3 1 \cdot \sqrt {\frac {8}{7}} \leq \frac {2}{5}. \\ \end{array}
$$

substituting into (8.2), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| y ^ {(i)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \leq \frac {4}{5} \| y ^ {(i - 1)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} + 2 \| \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(0)}) \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \\ \leq \frac {4}{5} \| y ^ {(i - 1)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} + 3 \| b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} \tag {8.3} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\frac{7}{8}\mathbf{m}^{-1} \preceq \mathbf{n}^{(i)} \preceq \frac{8}{7}\mathbf{m}^{-1}$  at the end. using  $(1 - \epsilon)\mathbf{m} \prec \mathbf{m}^{(k)} \prec (1 + \epsilon)\mathbf{m}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(0)} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} = \left\| \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1} b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {- 1} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} \left\| \mathbf {m} \left(\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}\right) ^ {- 1} b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \right\| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} \\ \leq (1 + \epsilon) \left(\| b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} + \| \mathbf {m} (\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) ^ {- 1} b - b \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}}\right) \\ \leq 2 \| b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} + 2 \epsilon \| b \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

putting this into (8.3), we have

$$
\| y ^ {(i)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \leq \frac {4}{5} \| y ^ {(i - 1)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} + 6 \| b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} + 6 \epsilon \| b \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}}.
$$

solving it, we have

$$
\| y ^ {(i)} - y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m}} \leq 3 0 \| b - \mathbf {m} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}} + 3 0 \epsilon \| b \| _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {- 1}}
$$

for all  $i$ . since  $\| y^{(i)}\|_{\mathbf{m}}$  is bounded uniformly,  $\mathbb{e}y^{(x)}$  exists. this also gives the bound for (8.1). now, we compute  $\mathbb{e}y^{(x)}$  and note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ y ^ {(x)} ] = \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \frac {1}{2} \mathbb {e} [ 2 (y ^ {(x - 1)} - \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \mathbf {n} ^ {(x - 1)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(x - 1)} y ^ {(x - 1)})) | x \geq 1 ] \\ = \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \mathbb {e} \left[ y ^ {(x - 1)} - \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \mathbf {n} ^ {(x - 1)} \left(b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(x - 1)} y ^ {(x - 1)}\right)\right) | x \geq 1 ] \\ = \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \mathbb {e} [ y ^ {(x)} - \frac {1}{2} y ^ {(0)} + \mathbf {n} ^ {(x)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(x)} y ^ {(x)}) ] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ y ^ {(x)} \right] + \mathbb {e} _ {i} \left[ \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}, \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)}, y ^ {(i)}} \left[ \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (b - \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} y ^ {(i)}) | x = i \right] \right] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ y ^ {(x)} \right] + \mathbb {e} _ {i} \left[ \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} | x = i \right] \left(b - \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} | x = i \right] \mathbb {e} \left[ y ^ {(i)} | x = i \right]\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {e} [ y ^ {(x)} ] + \mathbb {e} _ {i} \left[ \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {n} ] \left(b - \mathbf {m} \mathbb {e} \left[ y ^ {(i)} | x = i \right]\right) \right] \\ = \mathbb {e} [ y ^ {(x)} ] + \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {n} ] (b - \mathbf {m} \mathbb {e} [ y ^ {(x)} ]) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $x - 1$  subjects to  $x \geq 1$  has the same distribution as  $x$  in the third equality,  $\mathbf{n}^{(i)}, \mathbf{m}^{(i)}$  and  $y^{(i)}$  are independent on the fifth equality, we used that  $\mathbb{e}\mathbf{m}^{(i)} = \mathbf{m}$  and that  $\mathbf{n}^{(i)}$  are identically distributed on the sixth equality.

as  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{n}^{(x)}]$  is positive definite, this implies  $\mathbb{e}y^{(x)} = \mathbf{m}^{-1}b$ .

next we provide a general stability lemma about solving linear systems and then use this to prove theorem 10 and theorem 11. this lemma is mainly for convenience purpose as the vector maintenance and leverage score maintenance assume we solve the linear system  $\mathbf{h}y = b$  by some  $y = \psi b$  where  $\psi \approx \mathbf{h}^{-1}$ . the following lemma shows that this definition is same as  $\| y - \mathbf{h}^{-1}b\|_{\mathbf{h}}$  is small compared to  $\| b\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}$ .

lemma 43. let  $y = \mathbf{h}^{-1}(b + v) \in \mathbb{r}^d$  such that  $\|v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}} \leq \epsilon \|b\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}$  with  $0 \leq \epsilon \leq \frac{1}{80}$  for symmetric positive definite  $\mathbf{h} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  and vector  $b \in \mathbb{r}^d$ . there is a symmetric matrix  $\delta \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  such that  $y = (\mathbf{h} + \delta)^{-1} b \in \mathbb{r}^d$  and  $\mathbf{h} + \delta \approx_{20\epsilon} \mathbf{h}$ .

proof. note that the claim is trivial if  $\| v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}} = 0$  and thus we assume this is not the case. consequently, by the assumption on  $v$  we have  $\| b + v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}} > 0$  and can define the vectors  $\overline{v} = \frac{\mathbf{h}^{-1 / 2}v}{\|v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}\in \mathbb{r}^d$  and  $\overline{z} = \frac{\mathbf{h}^{-1 / 2}(b + v)}{\|b + v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}\in \mathbb{r}^d$  where  $s = \mathrm{sign}(b^\top \mathbf{h}^{-1}v)$ . we define

$$
\delta = s \frac {\| v \| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}}}{\| b + v \| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}}} \mathbf {h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\bar {z} z ^ {\top} - \frac {(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) (\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top}}{(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top} \bar {z}}\right) \mathbf {h} ^ {1 / 2}
$$

note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta y = \delta \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} (b + v) \\ = s \frac {\left\| v \right\| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}}}{\left\| b + v \right\| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}}} \mathbf {h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\bar {z} \bar {z} ^ {\top} - \frac {(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) (\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top}}{(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top} \bar {z}}\right) \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} (b + v) \\ = s \| v \| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}} \mathbf {h} ^ {1 / 2} \left(\bar {z} \bar {z} ^ {\top} - \frac {(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) (\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top}}{(\bar {z} + s \bar {v}) ^ {\top} \bar {z}}\right) \bar {z} \\ = s \| v \| _ {\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}} \mathbf {h} ^ {1 / 2} (\bar {z} - (\bar {z} + s \bar {v})) = - v. \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we have  $(\mathbf{h} + \delta)y = b + v - v = b$ . hence, we have  $y = (\mathbf{h} + \delta)^{-1}b$  if  $\mathbf{h} + \delta \succ 0$ . in fact, we now prove that  $\mathbf{h} + \delta \approx_{20\epsilon}\mathbf{h}$ .

first, we note that  $\| v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}\leq \frac{1}{2}\| b\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}$  and hence  $\frac{\|v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}{\|b + v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}\leq \frac{2\|v\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}{\|b\|_{\mathbf{h}^{-1}}}\leq 2\epsilon$ . hence, we have

$$
\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \delta \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \preceq 2 \epsilon \big (\overline {{z z}} ^ {\top} + \frac {(\overline {{z}} + s \overline {{v}}) (\overline {{z}} + s \overline {{v}}) ^ {\top}}{(\overline {{z}} + s \overline {{v}}) ^ {\top} \overline {{z}}} \big).
$$

the term  $\overline{z}\overline{z}^{\top}$  is simply bounded by i. we note that  $(\overline{z} + s\overline{v})(\overline{z} + s\overline{v})^{\top} \preceq \| \overline{z} + s\overline{v}\|^{2} \cdot \mathbf{i} \preceq 4 \cdot \mathbf{i}$ . finally, we note that  $(\overline{z} + s\overline{v})^{\top}\overline{z} \geq 1$  because  $s = \mathrm{sign}(\overline{v}^{\top}\overline{z})$ . hence, we have

$$
\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \delta \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \preceq 2 \epsilon \cdot 5 \cdot \mathbf {i} = 1 0 \epsilon \cdot \mathbf {i}.
$$

similarly, we have  $\mathbf{h}^{-1 / 2}\delta \mathbf{h}^{-1 / 2}\succeq -10\epsilon \cdot \mathbf{i}$ . hence, we have  $\mathbf{h} + \delta \approx_{20\epsilon}\mathbf{h}$

we now have all tools available to prove theorem 10.

proof of theorem 10. since the input  $\widetilde{w}^{(i)}\approx w^{(i)}$  of the algorithm is independent to the output of the algorithm in the previous iterations, every constraint  $i\in [n]$  has  $\theta (\tau_i(w)\epsilon^{-2}\gamma)$  probability of

being picked into  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\mathbf{a}$ . therefore, the expected number of rank changes in line 16 of update  $(\widetilde{w})$  is given by

$$
\theta (\gamma \epsilon^ {- 2}) \sum_ {j \in [ i _ {\ell} ]} \tau_ {j} (w) = \theta (2 ^ {\ell} \epsilon^ {- 2} \log (n)).
$$

for simplicity, we call such step, a rank  $2^{\ell}$  update. hence, the total complexity of  $\mathrm{update}(w)$  over  $k$  iterations is

$$
o (k n) + \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\lceil \log n \rceil} (\# \text {o f r a n k} 2 ^ {\ell} \text {u p d a t e s}) \cdot (\text {c o s t o f r a n k} 2 ^ {\ell} \text {u p d a t e}).
$$

note that a rank  $2^{\ell}$  update can be implemented in  $o(d^{2}(2^{\ell} \cdot \epsilon^{-2} \cdot \log(n))^{\omega - 2})$  time. now, we bound the number of such updates over  $t$  iterations.

the algorithm defines an error vector  $y_{i} \gets \frac{8}{\epsilon} (\widetilde{w}_{i} / \widetilde{w}_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})} - 1)$  and  $y_{i + n} \gets 2(\widetilde{\tau}_{i} / \widetilde{\tau}_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})} - 1)$ . we now also define the true error vector  $y_{i}^{*} \gets \frac{8}{\epsilon} (w_{i} / w_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})} - 1)$  and  $y_{i + n}^{*} \gets 2(\tau_{i} / \tau_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})} - 1)$  for  $i \in [n]$  where  $\tau_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  is updated to  $\tau_{i}$  whenever  $\widetilde{\tau}_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  is updated to  $\widetilde{\tau}_{i}$ , and likewise  $w_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  is updated to  $w_{i}$  whenever  $\widetilde{w}_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  is updated to  $\widetilde{w}_{i}$ . let  $y^{*(0)} \in \mathbb{r}^d$  be the true error vector defined by the algorithm directly after a rank  $2^{\ell}$  update. assume there is another rank  $2^{\ell}$  update after  $b$  iterations. let  $y^{*(1)}, y^{*(2)}, \dots, y^{*(j)}, \dots, y^{*(b)} \in \mathbb{r}^d$  be the true error vector after the  $j$ -th iteration for  $j = 1, \dots, b$ . we order the entries of each  $y^{*(j)}$  and  $y^{(j)}$  in the same way such that the entries of  $|y^{(b)}|$  (the error vector of the algorithm) are decreasing. let  $j_{i}$  be the last iteration  $w_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  (if  $i \leq n$ ) or  $\tau_{i - n}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$  (if  $i > n$ ) is updated after this first update. we set  $j_{i} = 0$  if it is never updated after the first update. if it is never updated, then we have  $|y_{i}^{*(j_{i})}| = |y_{i}^{*(0)}| \leq |y_{i}^{(0)}| + \frac{1}{8[\log d]} \leq 1 - \frac{\ell}{2[\log d]} + \frac{1}{8[\log d]}$  where  $1 - \frac{\ell}{2[\log d]}$  comes from the threshold for  $2^{\ell}$  update and  $\frac{1}{8[\log d]}$  comes from the error of  $\widetilde{\tau}$  and  $\widetilde{w}$  compared to  $\tau$  and  $w$ . if it is updated during the interval, we have  $|y_{i}^{*(j_{i})}| = \frac{1}{8[\log d]}$  (due to the error of  $\widetilde{\tau}$ ). in both cases, we have that

$$
\left| y _ {i} ^ {* \left(j _ {i}\right)} \right| \leq 1 - \frac {\ell}{2 \lceil \log d \rceil} + \frac {1}{8 \lceil \log d \rceil} \text {f o r a l l} i \in [ n ].
$$

on the other hand, that we are making a  $2^{\ell}$  update at step  $b$  implies it does not pass the threshold for a  $2^{\ell -1}$  update. hence, we have

$$
\left| y _ {i} ^ {* (b - 1)} \right| \geq 1 - \frac {\ell - 1}{2 \lceil \log d \rceil} - \frac {1}{8 \lceil \log d \rceil} \text {f o r a l l} i \leq i _ {\ell - 1},
$$

where  $i_{\ell}$  is the one defined in the  $b$ -th iteration. in particular, we have that  $|y_i^{*(b-1)} - y_i^{*(j_i)}| \geq \frac{1}{4[\log d]}$  for all  $i \leq i_{\ell-1}$ . let  $\tau_i^{(j)}$  be the shifted leverage score  $\tau(w)$  before the  $j$ -th iteration. then, by the definition of  $i_{\ell-1}$ , we have

$$
\omega \left(\frac {1}{\log^ {2} d}\right) \cdot 2 ^ {\ell - 1} \leq \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \tau_ {i} ^ {(b - 1)} \left(y _ {i} ^ {* (b - 1)} - y _ {i} ^ {* (j _ {i})}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {8.4}
$$

next, we note that the denominator of  $y_{i}^{*(j)}$  never changed between  $j_{i}$  and  $b$ . hence,  $y_{i}^{*(j + 1)} - y_{i}^{*(j)}$

is exactly equals to the relative change of  $w$  or  $\tau$ . hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \tau_ {i} ^ {(b - 1)} \left(y _ {i} ^ {* (b - 1)} - y _ {i} ^ {* (j _ {i})}\right) ^ {2} \leq o (b) \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \tau_ {i} ^ {(b - 1)} \sum_ {j = j _ {i}} ^ {b - 1} \left(y _ {i} ^ {* (j + 1)} - y _ {i} ^ {* (j)}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq o (b) \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \sum_ {j = j _ {i}} ^ {b - 1} \tau_ {i} ^ {(j)} \left(y _ {i} ^ {* (j + 1)} - y _ {i} ^ {* (j)}\right) ^ {2} \\ = o \left(b ^ {2}\right) \tag {8.5} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\widetilde{\tau}_i^{(j)}$  (and thus also  $\tau_i^{(j)}$ ) does not change more than a constant from iteration  $j_{i}$  to iteration  $b - 1$  in the second inequality and the assumption (4.1) at the end. from (8.4) and (8.5), we see that there is at least  $b\geq \omega (\frac{2^{\ell / 2}}{\log d})$  many iterations between two  $2^{\ell}$  rank updates. hence, the total complexity of  $\mathrm{update}(w)$  over  $k$  iterations is

$$
o (k n) + \sum_ {\ell = 0} ^ {\lceil \log d \rceil} (\frac {k \log d}{2 ^ {\ell / 2}}) \cdot d ^ {2} (\epsilon^ {- 2} 2 ^ {\ell} \log (n)) ^ {\omega - 2} = o (k n + \epsilon^ {- 2} k \cdot (d ^ {\omega - (1 / 2)} + d ^ {2}) \cdot \log^ {3 / 2} (n)).
$$

the cost of solve is clear from the description.

for the correctness of update, we note that the internal  $\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{alg})}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  and the input  $\widetilde{w}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  satisfies  $\left|\frac{\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{alg})} - \widetilde{w}}{\widetilde{w}}\right|\leq \frac{\epsilon}{4}$  and that  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\mathbf{a}\approx_{\epsilon /4}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}^{(\mathrm{alg})}\mathbf{a}$  with probability  $1 - 1 / \mathrm{poly}(nk)$  due to the matrix chernoff bound (see e.g. the proof of lemma 4 in [clm  $^+$  15]). here, we used the fact that the input  $\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{new})}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  is independent of the algorithm output and hence each sample of  $v_{i}$  are independent. hence, we have  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{v}\mathbf{a}\approx_{\epsilon}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}\mathbf{a}$  with probability  $1 - 1 / \mathrm{poly}(n)$ .

for the correctness of solve, assume for now that  $\psi$  in line 27 satisfies  $\psi^{-1} = (1\pm 1 / 8)\mathbf{a}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{w}}\mathbf{a}$  and that  $y^{(0)}$  is a good approximation of the solution with

$$
\left\| y ^ {(0)} - \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} b \right\| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right)} \leq (\delta / 1 8 0 0) \cdot \| b \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}}.
$$

(this is shown in the proof of lemma 11.) so lemma 42 shows that the final result  $y$  satisfies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| b - \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} y \right\| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} \leq 3 0 \| b - \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} y ^ {(0)} \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} + (\delta / 6 0) \| b \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} \\ \leq \delta / 3 0 \| b \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}}. \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, lemma 43 shows that we can view the output of the algorithm as  $\psi b$  for some spectral approximation  $\psi \approx_{\delta} \mathbf{a}^{\top} \overline{\mathbf{w}} \mathbf{a}$ .

lemma 44. theorem 10 holds even if the input  $w$  and  $\widetilde{\tau}$  of the algorithm depends on the output of solve. furthermore, we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {k \in [ k - 1 ]} \left\| \sqrt {\widetilde {\mathbf {w}} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g}) (k + 1)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\widetilde {\mathbf {w}} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g}) (k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\widetilde {\mathbf {w}} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g}) (k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\widetilde {\mathbf {w}} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g}) (k)}} \right\| _ {f} \right] \leq 1 6 k \log^ {5 / 2} n
$$

where  $\psi^{(k)}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$ ,  $\widetilde{w}^{(\mathrm{alg})(k)}$  is the output of the  $k$ -th step of update  $(\widetilde{w}^{(k)},\widetilde{\tau}^{(k)})$ .

proof. the output  $y \in \mathbb{r}^d$  of the function securesolve solves satisfies

$$
y = \psi_ {\text {s o l v e}} (b + \alpha \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} ^ {1 / 2} \eta) \tag {8.6}
$$

with  $\alpha = \frac{c_3\epsilon}{\sqrt{d\log(n / \delta)}}\| y_1\|_{\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{u}\mathbf{a}}$  for some  $\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}$  satisfying  $\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}\approx_{\mathrm{poly}(\delta /n)}(\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{u}\mathbf{a})^{-1}$  as the algorithm performs iterative refinement on our preconditioner  $\psi$  maintained by update. note that

$$
\alpha^ {2} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} ^ {1 / 2} \eta \| _ {\psi_ {\text {s o l v e}}} ^ {2} \leq 2 \alpha^ {2} \eta^ {\top} \mathbf {u} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} ^ {1 / 2} \eta = o (\alpha^ {2} \log (n) d)
$$

w.h.p. in  $n$  where we used that  $\mathbf{u}^{1/2}\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{a}^\top\mathbf{u}\mathbf{a})^{-1}\mathbf{a}^\top\mathbf{u}^{1/2}$  is a rank  $d$  orthogonal projection matrix and  $\eta \sim n(0, \mathbf{i}_n)$ . by choosing small enough  $c_3$ , we have

$$
\alpha \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u} ^ {1 / 2} \eta \| _ {\psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}}} \leq \frac {\delta}{2 0 0} \| y _ {1} \| _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a}} = \frac {\delta}{2 0 0} \| \psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}} b \| _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a}} \leq \frac {\delta}{1 0 0} \| b \| _ {\psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}}}.
$$

by picking small enough  $c_{3}$ , lemma 43 shows that we have  $y = (\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}^{-1} + \delta)^{-1}b$  for  $\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}^{-1} + \delta \approx_{\delta /2}$ $\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}^{-1}\approx_{\delta}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\overline{\mathbf{w}}\mathbf{a}$ .

note that we also have

$$
y \sim n (\psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}} b, \alpha^ {2} \psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a} \psi_ {\mathrm {s o l v e}}).
$$

on the other hand, the output  $y \in \mathbb{r}^d$  of the function idealsolve satisfies

$$
y ^ {(\mathrm {i d e a l})} \sim n ((\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a}) ^ {- 1} b, \alpha^ {2} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {u a}) ^ {- 1}).
$$

since  $\psi_{\mathrm{solve}}^{-1} \approx_{\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon/(nk))} \mathbf{a}^{\top} \mathbf{u} \mathbf{a}$ , the total variation between  $y$  and  $y^{(\mathrm{ideal})} \in \mathbb{r}^d$  is  $\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon/(nk))$  small.

now consider what happens when we replace the matrix  $\psi$  in lines 27 with the function idealsolve. note that theorem 10 then holds when the input  $w\in \mathbb{r}^n$  and  $\widetilde{\tau}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  of the algorithm depends on the output of solve because solve and idealsolve do not use  $v$  and hence cannot leak any information about  $v$ . if theorem 10 does not hold with input depending on solve, when we replace the matrix  $\psi$  in lines 27 with the function securesolve instead, then this would give an algorithm to distinguish the functions idealsolve and securesolve with probability at least  $\mathrm{poly}(\epsilon /(nk))$ . this contradicts with the total variation between  $y$  and  $y^{(\mathrm{ideal})}\in \mathbb{r}^d$ .

for the frobenius bound we will write for simplicity  $\mathbf{w}^{(k)}$  for  $\widetilde{\mathbf{w}}^{(\mathrm{alg})}$ . we note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {k} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ = 3 \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ + 3 \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ + 3 \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ = 3 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ (\sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}) ^ {2} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} \right] \\ + 3 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \left(\psi^ {(k + 1)} - \psi^ {(k)}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} \left(\psi^ {(k + 1)} - \psi^ {(k)}\right) \right] \\ + 3 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ (\sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}) ^ {2} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \right] \\ \leq 8 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ (\sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)} / \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} - \mathbf {i}) ^ {2} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \boldsymbol {\psi} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right] \\ + 4 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \left(\psi^ {(k + 1)} - \psi^ {(k)}\right) \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} \left(\psi^ {(k + 1)} - \psi^ {(k)}\right) \right] \\ \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} e _ {k} ^ {(1)} + e _ {k} ^ {(2)} \tag {8.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\psi^{(k + 1)}\preceq \frac{9}{8}\psi^{(k)}$  and  $\mathbf{w}^{(k + 1)}\preceq \frac{9}{8}\mathbf{w}^{(k)}$  in the first inequality.

for the first term, we use  $\psi^{(k)}\preceq \frac{9}{8} (\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{(k)}\mathbf{a})^{-1}$ ,  $\mathbf{p}^{(k)}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\sqrt{\mathbf{w}^{(k)}}\mathbf{a}(\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{w}^{(k)}\mathbf{a})^{-1}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\sqrt{\mathbf{w}^{(k)}}$  and  $|\sqrt{x} -1|\leq |x - 1|$  for  $\frac{1}{2}\leq x\leq 2$  and get

$$
e _ {k} ^ {(1)} \leq 3 0 \cdot \operatorname {t r} \left[ \left(\frac {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}{\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}\right) ^ {2} \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \right] = 3 0 \cdot \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {w _ {i} ^ {(k + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}\right) ^ {2}. \tag {8.8}
$$

for the second term, we note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \psi^ {(k + 1)} - \psi^ {(k)} = (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} - (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \\ = \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}) \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} d s \\ \end{array}
$$

where  $\mathbf{h}_s = \mathbf{a}^\top \left((1 - s)\mathbf{v}^{(k)} + s\mathbf{v}^{(k + 1)}\right)\mathbf{a}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$ . let  $\mathbf{d}_v^{(k)} = \frac{\mathbf{v}^{(k + 1)} - \mathbf{v}^{(k)}}{\mathbf{w}^{(k)}}\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times n}$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} e _ {k} ^ {(2)} = 4 \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \int_ {0} ^ {1} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}\right) \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} d s \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ \leq 4 \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}\right) \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} d s\right) ^ {2} \\ = 4 \left(\int_ {0} ^ {1} \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \left(\frac {\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}}{\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}\right) \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} _ {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} d s\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq 8 \left\| \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}}{\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}\right) \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\mathbf{h}_s^{-1} \preceq \frac{9}{8} (\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{w}^{(k)}\mathbf{a})^{-1}$  at the end. note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ e ^ {(2)} \right] = 8 \mathbb {e} \left[ \operatorname {t r} \left[ \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}}{\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}\right) \mathbf {p} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {\mathbf {v} ^ {(k + 1)} - \mathbf {v} ^ {(k)}}{\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}}\right) \right] \right] \\ = 8 \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \sum_ {j \in [ n ]} (\mathbf {p} ^ {(k)}) _ {i, j} ^ {2} \cdot \frac {v _ {i} ^ {(k + 1)} - v _ {i} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}} \cdot \frac {v _ {j} ^ {(k + 1)} - v _ {j} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {j} ^ {(k)}} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

and for each  $i$ ,  $\mathbb{p}(v_i^{(k)} \neq 0) = \min \{1, \gamma \epsilon^{-2} \cdot \widetilde{\tau}_i^{(\mathrm{alg}, k)}\}$ . also, this is independent to whether  $v_j^{(k)} \neq 0$ . using the formula of  $v$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \frac {v _ {i} ^ {(k + 1)} - v _ {i} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}} \cdot \frac {v _ {j} ^ {(k + 1)} - v _ {j} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {j} ^ {(k)}} \right] \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {2}{\min \{1 , \gamma \epsilon^ {- 2} \cdot \widetilde {\tau} _ {i} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g}, k)} \}} \cdot \frac {(w _ {i} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g} , k)}) ^ {2}}{(w _ {i} ^ {(k)}) ^ {2}} & \text {i f} i = j \\ 4 \cdot \frac {w _ {i} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g} , k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}} \cdot \frac {w _ {j} ^ {(\mathrm {a l g} , k)}}{w _ {j} ^ {(k)}} & \text {i f} i \neq j \end{array} \right. \\ \leq \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {1 6}{\gamma \epsilon^ {- 2 .} \tau_ {i} ^ {(k)}} & \text {i f} i = j \\ 1 6 & \text {i f} i \neq j \end{array} \right. \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $w_{i}^{(\mathrm{alg},k)}\leq 2w_{i}^{(k)}$  and  $\tau_i^{(k)}\geq \frac{1}{2}\widetilde{\tau}_i^{(\mathrm{alg},k)}$

let  $\mathcal{i}_k = \{i: w_i^{(\mathrm{alg}, k + 1)} \neq w_i^{(\mathrm{alg}, k)}\}$  be the indices that the algorithm resampled at iteration  $k$ . then, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ e _ {k} ^ {(2)} \right] \leq 1 6 \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}, j \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \left(\mathbf {p} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {i, j} ^ {2} + 1 6 \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \frac {\left(\mathbf {p} ^ {(k)}\right) _ {i , i} ^ {2}}{\gamma \epsilon^ {- 2} \cdot \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)}} \\ \leq 1 6 \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \sum_ {j \in [ n ]} (\mathbf {p} ^ {(k)}) _ {i, j} ^ {2} + \frac {1 6 \epsilon^ {2}}{\gamma} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \mathbf {p} _ {i, i} ^ {(k)} \\ = 1 6 \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} + \frac {1 6 \epsilon^ {2}}{\gamma} \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \leq 3 2 \sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \tag {8.9} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the fact  $\sum_{j\in [n]}(\mathbf{p}^{(k)})_{i,j}^2 = \sigma_i^{(k)}$  for all  $i$ . finally, we note that by the choice of the indices to update, we have

$$
\sum_ {i \in \mathcal {i} _ {k}} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \leq 2 ^ {\ell_ {k}} + 1 \tag {8.10}
$$

where  $\ell_{k}$  is defined in the  $k$ -th step of update. hence, we have  $\mathbb{e}[e_k^{(2)}] \leq 64 \cdot 2^{\ell_k}$ . using this, (8.7) and (8.8), we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k + 1)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k + 1)}} - \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \mathbf {a} \psi^ {(k)} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mathbf {w} ^ {(k)}} \right\| _ {f} \right] \\ \leq \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} \sqrt {e _ {k} ^ {(1)}} + \sqrt {e _ {k} ^ {(2)}} \right] \\ \leq 6 \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {w _ {i} ^ {(k + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}\right) ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} + 8 \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} 2 ^ {\ell_ {k} / 2} \\ \leq 6 \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} \left(\sum_ {i = 1} ^ {n} \sigma_ {i} ^ {(k)} \left(\frac {w _ {i} ^ {(k + 1)} - w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}{w _ {i} ^ {(k)}}\right) ^ {2}\right) ^ {1 / 2} + 8 \sum_ {k = 0} ^ {k - 1} 2 ^ {\ell_ {k} / 2} \\ \leq 6 k + 8 k \log^ {2} n \leq 1 6 k \log^ {2} n \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used the fact that a rank  $2^{\ell}$  update happens at most once every  $\frac{2^{\ell/2}}{\log n}$  steps (proved in theorem 10).

# 9 open problems

our main result is a linear program solver that runs in expected  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(nd + d^3)$  time. for dense constraint matrices  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$  this is optimal among algorithms that do not use fast matrix multiplication, barring a major improvement in solving linear systems. the fastest linear system solvers run in  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(\mathrm{nnz}(a) + d^\omega)$  time, where  $\mathrm{nnz}(a)$  is the number of nonzero entries in  $\mathbf{a}$  and  $\omega$  is the matrix exponent. this leads to two open questions: (i) can the  $nd$  term in our complexity be improved to  $\mathrm{nnz}(a)$ ? (ii) can the  $d^3$  term be improved to  $d^\omega$  by exploiting fast matrix multiplication?

a major bottleneck for question (i) is how to detect large entries of the product  $\mathbf{a}h$  for  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$ ,  $h \in \mathbb{r}^d$  (i.e. solving the problem of lemma 33). currently the complexity of lemma 33 is  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(\| \mathbf{g} \mathbf{a} h\|^2 \cdot \varepsilon^{-2} \cdot d)$ , which can be interpreted as "  $d$  times the number of entries larger than  $\varepsilon$ " note that verifying the answer (that is, for a list of indices  $i \subset [n]$ , check that  $(\mathbf{a}h)_i$  is indeed larger than  $\varepsilon$ ) requires the same complexity for dense matrices  $\mathbf{a}$ . however, for matrices with  $z \leq d$  entries per row, the verification complexity is just  $z \cdot |i|$ . this suggests that it might be possible to improve lemma 33 to run in  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(\| \mathbf{g} \mathbf{a} h\|^2 \cdot \varepsilon^{-2} \cdot z)$  for matrices  $\mathbf{a}$  with  $z$  nonzero entries per row. if such a data structure could be found, it would result in a faster linear programming algorithm.

so far question (ii) has only been answered for the case  $d = \omega(n)$  [cls19, bra20] and no progress has been made for the more general case  $d = o(n)$ , even when allowing for a worse dependency on  $n$  than our  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(nd + d^3)$  bound. so far the best dependency on  $d$  is  $d^{2.5} \gg d^\omega[\mathrm{ls14}, \mathrm{ls15}]$ , so a first step could be to improve our complexity to  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(nd + d^{2.5})$  time. currently the  $d^3$  bottleneck comes from maintaining the feasibility of the primal solution  $x$  in section b, so a key step would be to improve the complexity of maintaining the feasibility.

# a misc technical lemmas

here we state various technical lemmas from prior work that we use throughout our paper.

lemma 45 (projection matrix facts (lemma 47 of [ls19])). let  $\mathbf{p} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times n}$  be an arbitrary orthogonal projection matrix and let  $\boldsymbol{\sigma} = \mathbf{diag}(\mathbf{p})$  and  $\mathbf{p}^{(2)} = \mathbf{p} \circ \mathbf{p}$ . for all  $i, j \in [m]$ ,  $x, y \in \mathbb{r}^m$ , and  $\mathbf{x} = \mathbf{diag}(x)$  we have

(1)  $\pmb{\sigma}_{ii} = \sum_{j\in [m]}\mathbf{p}_{ij}^{(2)}$

(5)  $\| \pmb{\sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{p}^{(2)}x\|_{\infty}\leq \| x\|_{\infty}$

(2)  $\mathbf{0} \preceq \mathbf{p}^{(2)} \preceq \mathbf{\sigma} \preceq \mathbf{i}$ , (in particular,  $0 \leq \mathbf{\sigma}_{ii} \leq 1$ )

(6)  $\sum_{i\in [n]}\pmb{\sigma}_{ii} = \mathrm{rank}(\mathbf{p})$

(3)  $\mathbf{p}_{ij}^{(2)}\leq \pmb{\sigma}_{ii}\pmb{\sigma}_{jj}$

(7)  $\left| y^\top \mathbf{x} \mathbf{p}^{(2)} y \right| \leq \| y \|_{\sigma}^2 \cdot \| x \|_{\sigma}$

(4)  $\| \pmb{\sigma}^{-1}\mathbf{p}^{(2)}x\|_{\infty}\leq \| x\|_{\pmb{\sigma}}$

(8)  $\left| y^\top (\mathbf{p} \circ \mathbf{p}\mathbf{x}\mathbf{p})y \right| \leq \| y\|_{\sigma}^2 \cdot \| x\|_{\sigma}$ .

lemma 46 (derivative of projection matrix (lemma 49 of [ls19])). given full rank  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$  and  $w \in \mathbb{r}_{>0}^n$  we have

$$
d _ {w} \mathbf {p} (\mathbf {w a}) [ h ] = \delta \mathbf {p} (\mathbf {w a}) + \mathbf {p} (\mathbf {w a}) \delta - 2 \mathbf {p} (\mathbf {w a}) \delta \mathbf {p} (\mathbf {w a})
$$

where  $\mathbf{w} = \mathbf{diag}(w)$ ,  $\delta = \mathbf{diag}(h / w)$ , and  $d_{w}f(w)[h]$  denote the directional derivative of  $f$  with respect to  $w$  in direction  $h$ . in particular, we have that

$$
d _ {w} \sigma (\mathbf {w}) [ h ] = 2 \boldsymbol {\lambda} (\mathbf {w}) \mathbf {w} ^ {- 1} h.
$$

the next lemma gives a variety of frequently used relationships between different types of multiplicative approximations.

lemma 47. let  $a, b \in \mathbb{r}_{>0}^n$  and  $\mathbf{a} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{diag}(a)$  and  $\mathbf{b} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{diag}(b)$ . if  $a \approx_{\epsilon} b$  for  $\epsilon \in (0,1/2)$  then

$$
\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} (a - b) \| _ {\infty} \leq \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2} a n d \| \mathbf {b} ^ {- 1} (a - b) \| _ {\infty} \leq \epsilon + \epsilon^ {2}.
$$

further, if for  $\epsilon \in (0,1 / 2)$  and either

$$
\| \mathbf {a} ^ {- 1} (a - b) \| _ {\infty} \leq \epsilon o r \| \mathbf {b} ^ {- 1} (a - b) \| _ {\infty} \leq \epsilon
$$

then  $a\approx_{\epsilon +\epsilon^2}b$

proof. these follow near immediately by taylor expansion.

![](images/7a05bde08789aba6ea2d38bd004b316afbe1559bdb66d25b8cf442e2d85d129e.jpg)

here we provide some helpful expectation bounds. this lemma just shows that the relative change of a matrix in frobenius norm can be bounded by the change in the projection schur product norm.

lemma 48 (matrix stability). for full column rank  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$  and all  $\delta \in \mathbb{r}^n$  we have

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} = \| \delta \| _ {\mathbf {p} ^ {(2)}}
$$

where  $\delta \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{diag}(\delta)$  and  $\mathbf{p} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{p}(\mathbf{a})$ .

proof. direct calculation yields that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \right\| _ {f} ^ {2} = \operatorname {t r} \left[ \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {p} \delta \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \right] \\ = \operatorname {t r} \left[ \mathbf {p} \delta \mathbf {p} \delta \right] \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \sum_ {j \in [ n ]} \left[ \mathbf {p} \delta \right] _ {i j} \left[ \mathbf {p} \delta \right] _ {j i} \\ = \sum_ {i, j \in [ n ]} \mathbf {p} _ {i j} ^ {2} \delta_ {i} \delta_ {j} \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/9585044c879e6896faa7b38e2e50d4ae050edc91eb6cc890bf2ad1dc6b6cb28d.jpg)

the following lemma (coupled with the one above) shows that we can leverage score sample to have small change in frobenius norm.

lemma 49 (expected frobenius). let  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$  be non-degenerate and  $\delta \in \mathbb{r}^n$ . further let  $\tau \geq \sigma(\mathbf{a})$  and let  $\widetilde{\delta} \in \mathbb{r}^n$  be chosen randomly where for all  $i \in [n]$  independently  $\widetilde{\delta}_i = \delta_i / p_i$  with probability  $p_i$  and  $\widetilde{\delta}_i = 0$  otherwise where  $p_i = \min\{1, \tau_i \cdot k\}$  for some  $k \geq 0$ . then

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \widetilde {\delta} ] = \delta a n d \mathbb {e} [ \| \widetilde {\delta} \| _ {\mathbf {p} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {a})} ^ {2} ] \leq (1 + (1 / k)) \cdot \| \delta \| _ {\sigma} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. clearly  $\mathbb{e}[\widetilde{\delta}] = \delta$  by construction. further, since the different entries of  $\widetilde{\delta}_i$  are set independently we have that  $\mathbb{e}[\widetilde{\delta}_i\widetilde{\delta}_j] = \delta_i\delta_j$  when  $i\neq j$ . consequently, letting  $\mathbf{p}$  therefore

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \| \widetilde {\delta} \| _ {\mathbf {p} ^ {(2)} (\mathbf {a})} ^ {2} ] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \sum_ {i, j \in [ n ]} \mathbf {p} _ {i, j} ^ {2} \delta_ {i} \delta_ {j} \right] \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \mathbf {p} _ {i, i} ^ {2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \widetilde {\delta} _ {i} ^ {2} \right] + \sum_ {i, j \in [ n ]: i \neq j} \mathbf {p} _ {i, j} ^ {2} \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {i} \delta_ {j} \right] \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \sigma_ {i} ^ {2} \cdot (\mathbb {e} [ \widetilde {\delta} _ {i} ^ {2} ] - (\mathbb {e} [ \widetilde {\delta} _ {i} ]) ^ {2}) + \| \delta \| _ {\mathbf {p} ^ {(2)}} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

now, either  $p_i = 1$  and

$$
\sigma_ {i} ^ {2} \cdot (\mathbb {e} [ \widetilde {\delta} _ {i} ^ {2} ] - (\mathbb {e} [ \widetilde {\delta} _ {i} ]) ^ {2}) = \sigma_ {i} \cdot (\delta_ {i} ^ {2} - \delta_ {i} ^ {2}) = 0
$$

or  $p_i < 1$  and  $p_i = k \cdot \tau_i \geq k \cdot \sigma_i$  in which case

$$
\sigma_ {i} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ \widetilde {\delta_ {i} ^ {2}} \right] - \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ \widetilde {\delta_ {i}} \right]\right) ^ {2}\right) \leq \frac {\sigma_ {i} ^ {2} \delta_ {i} ^ {2}}{p _ {i}} \leq \frac {\sigma_ {i}}{k} \cdot \delta_ {i} ^ {2}.
$$

combining these facts and leveraging that  $\mathbf{p}^{(2)}\preceq \pmb{\sigma}$  (lemma 45) then yields the result.

# b maintaining near feasibility

in this section we prove the remainder of theorem 5. note that when assuming the interior point  $x \in \mathbb{r}^n$  stays feasible, we have proven theorem 5 by theorem 32. thus we are only left with analyzing how to make sure that  $x$  stays approximately feasible. this is required because the step  $\delta_x$  performed by our ipm does not necessarily satisfy  $\mathbf{a}^\top \delta_x = 0$ , due to the spectral approximation we used for  $(\mathbf{a}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}} \mathbf{s}^{-1} \mathbf{a})^{-1}$  in our steps.

given some infeasible  $x$  we can obtain a feasible  $x'$  via

$$
x ^ {\prime} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} x - \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b),
$$

we show in lemma 56 that these  $x$  and  $x'$  are close multiplicatively

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} (x ^ {\prime} - x) \| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {2} \approx \mu^ {- 1} \| x - x ^ {\prime} \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2}
$$

which motivates that we want to minimize the term on the right. hence we measure the infeasibility of a point  $x \in \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n$  by the potential  $\phi_b: \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n \times \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n \times \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n \times \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0} \to \mathbb{r}$ , where for any  $x', s' \in \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n$

$$
\phi_ {b} \left(x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu\right) = \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2}. \tag {b.1}
$$

here we prove that throughout the algorithm, we maintain  $\phi_b \leq \frac{\zeta \epsilon^2}{\log^6 n}$ , where  $\epsilon$  is the parameter of theorem 5 and  $\zeta > 0$  is a sufficiently small constant.

the extra parameters  $x', s' \in \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n$  of our potential function  $\phi_b$  are motivated by the fact, that we analyze this norm in several steps. our proof is split into three parts. first, we analyze how much the potential  $\phi_b(x + \delta_x, x', s', \mu)$  increases by performing the ipm step  $\delta_x$  in section b.1. second, we analyze how much the potential increases by moving  $x'$ ,  $s'$  to  $x' + \delta_x$ ,  $s' + \delta_s$  in section b.2. in general, this could incur a constant factor increase of the potential, so we modify the ipm to also perform a small corrective step  $\delta_x'$  and analyze  $\phi_b(x + \delta_x + \delta_x', x' + \delta_{x'}, s' + \delta_{s'}, \mu)$  instead. in section b.3 we show that it takes  $\widetilde{\omega}(\sqrt{d})$  iterations of the modified ipm until  $\phi_b$  increases by a constant factor. after such  $\widetilde{\omega}(\sqrt{d})$  iterations, the algorithm then performs another more expensive correction step, which decreases the potential again. this way the potential will stay small throughout the entire ipm, which then concludes the proof of theorem 5.

# b.1 increase of infeasibility due to  $x$

first, we show that  $\phi_b$  increases slowly when the step  $\delta_x$  of the ipm is correct in expectation (that is,  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{a}\delta_x] = 0$ ). in particular, it takes  $\sqrt{d}$  iterations to increase  $\phi_b$  by 1.

lemma 50 (infeasibility change due to  $x$ ). assume that  $\overline{\mathbf{x}}\overline{s} \approx_1 \mu \overline{\tau} \in \mathbb{r}^n$ ,  $x' \approx_1 x \approx_1 \overline{x}$ ,  $s' \approx_1 s \approx_1 \overline{s}$  and  $\overline{\tau} \approx_1 \tau(\overline{x}, \overline{s}) \in \mathbb{r}^n$ . let  $\overline{\mathbf{q}} = \mathbf{a}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$ . let  $\mathbf{h} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  be a random symmetric matrix satisfying

$$
\mathbf {h} \approx_ {\epsilon_ {h}} \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} a n d \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} ] = \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} ^ {- 1}.
$$

for some  $\epsilon_h\in (0,1)$ . further, let  $x^{(\mathrm{new})} = x + \delta_x$  where  $\delta_{x} = \overline{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{w}}^{-1 / 2}(\mathbf{i} - \mathbf{p})\overline{\mathbf{w}}^{1 / 2}h\in \mathbb{r}^n$  with  $\overline{\mathbf{w}} = \overline{\mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}}\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times n}$  and  $\mathbf{p} = \overline{\mathbf{s}}^{-1 / 2}\overline{\mathbf{x}}^{1 / 2}\mathbf{a}\mathbf{h}^{-1}\mathbf{a}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}}^{1 / 2}\overline{\mathbf{s}}^{-1 / 2}$ . then

$$
\mathbb {e} _ {\delta_ {x}} [ \phi_ {b} (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) ] \leq \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) + o (\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}) \cdot \| h \| _ {\overline {{\tau}}} ^ {2}.
$$

proof. note that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta_ {x} \right] = \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {i} - \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} ^ {1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \overline {{\mathbf {w}}} ^ {1 / 2} h = 0. \tag {b.2}
$$

for easy of analysis, we define matrix  $\mathbf{q}' \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  as  $\mathbf{q}' \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}'\mathbf{s}'^{-1}\mathbf{a}$ . hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \phi_ {b} (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) ] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (x + \delta_ {x}) - b \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (x + \delta_ {x}) - b \| _ {(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (x + \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]) - b \| _ {(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} + \mathbb {e} \left[ \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]) \| _ {(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right] \\ = \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) + \mathbb {e} \left[ \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]) \| _ {(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \right]. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second step follows from  $\mathbb{e}[x^2] = (\mathbb{e}[x])^2 + \mathbb{e}[(x - \mathbb{e}[x])^2]$ , and the last step follows from (b.1) (the definition of  $\phi_b$ ) and (b.2).

next, we note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ] = \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} - (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}) \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \\ = \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} - (\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}) \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h. \\ \end{array}
$$

we define matrix  $\mathbf{m} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  as follows

$$
\mathbf {m} \stackrel {\text {d e f}} {=} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {1 / 2} = \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} ^ {1 / 2}.
$$

since  $\mathbf{h} \approx_{\epsilon_h} \mathbf{a}^\top \overline{\mathbf{s}}^{-1} \overline{\mathbf{x}} \mathbf{a}$ , we have  $\| \mathbf{m} - \mathbf{i} \|_2 = o(\epsilon_h)$ . further  $(\mathbf{q}')^{-1} = (\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}' \mathbf{s}'^{-1} \mathbf{a})^{-1} \approx_{o(1)} (\mathbf{a}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}} \mathbf{s}^{-1} \mathbf{a})^{-1} = (\overline{\mathbf{q}})^{-1}$ , hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]\right) \right\| _ {\left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \leq o (1) \cdot \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]\right) \right\| _ {\left(\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}\right) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \overline {{\mathbf {q}}} (\mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} - (\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}) \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot \| (\mathbf {m} - \mathbf {i}) (\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \| \mathbf {m} - \mathbf {i} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| (\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \left\| (\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \right\| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third step follows from definition of matrix  $\mathbf{m}$ , the forth step follows from  $\| \mathbf{a}u\| _2\leq$ $\| \mathbf{a}\| _2\cdot \| u\| _2$  the fifth step follows from  $\| \mathbf{m} - \mathbf{i}\| _2 = o(\epsilon_h)$ . hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mu^ {- 1} \cdot \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\delta_ {x} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {x} ]\right) \| _ {(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \leq o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} / \mu\right) \cdot \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} h \| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} / \mu\right) \cdot \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}}} \sqrt {\overline {{\mathbf {s x}}}} h \right\| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {q}}}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} / \mu\right) \cdot \left\| \sqrt {\overline {{\mathbf {s x}}}} h \right\| _ {\mathbf {p} (\overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1 / 2} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a})} ^ {2} \\ \leq o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} / \mu\right) \cdot \left\| \sqrt {\mathbf {s x}} h \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step follows from properties of projection matrices. as  $\overline{\mathbf{x}}\overline{s}\approx_1\mu \overline{\tau}$  and  $\overline{\tau}\approx_{1}\tau (\overline{x},\overline{s})$  the result follows.

algorithm 10: maintaining infeasibility   
1 procedure maintaininfeasibility  $(x\in \mathbb{r}^n,s\in \mathbb{r}^n,\overline{\tau}\in \mathbb{r}^n)$    
2 let  $x^{\prime}\in \mathbb{r}^{n},s^{\prime}\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  be the previous input to maintaininfeasibility.   
3 let  $\mathbf{q} = \mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}$  and  $\mathbf{q}' = \mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}'\mathbf{s}'^{-1}\mathbf{a}$    
4 generate independent random  $\mathbf{h}_1\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  such that  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{h}_1^{-1}] = \mathbf{q}^{-1}$  and  $\mathbf{h}_1\approx_{\epsilon_h}\mathbf{q}$    
5 generate independent random  $\mathbf{h}_3\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  such that  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{h}_3^{-1}] = \mathbf{q}'^{-1}$  and  $\mathbf{h}_3\approx_{\epsilon_h}\mathbf{q}'$    
6 generate independent random  $\mathbf{h}_2\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  such that; // lemma 52   
7  $\begin{array}{r}\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{h}_2] = \mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}^{1 / 2}\mathbf{s}^{-1 / 2}(\mathbf{x}^{1 / 2}\mathbf{s}^{-1 / 2} - \mathbf{x}^{\prime 1 / 2}\mathbf{s}^{\prime -1 / 2})\mathbf{a},\\ \mathbb{e}[\| \mathbf{q}^{\prime -1 / 2}\mathbf{h}_2\mathbf{q}^{\prime -1 / 2}\| _f^2 ] = o(\| \ln x' - \ln x\|_{\tau (x,s)}^2 +\| \ln s' - \ln s\|_{\tau (x,s)}^2) \end{array}$  and   
8 generate independent random  $\mathbf{h}_4\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  such that; // lemma 52   
9  $\begin{array}{rl} & {\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{h}_4] = \mathbf{q}^{-1} - \mathbf{q}'^{-1},}\\ & {\mathbb{e}[\| \mathbf{q}^{\prime 1 / 2}\mathbf{h}_4\mathbf{q}^{\prime 1 / 2}\| _f^2 ] = o(\| \ln x' - \ln x\|_{\tau (x,s)}^2 +\| \ln s' - \ln s\|_{\tau (x,s)}^2) \text{and}}\\ & {\mathbf{h}_4 = \mathbb{e}[\mathbf{h}_4]\pm \epsilon_h\cdot \mathbf{q}'^{-1}.} \end{array}$    
these matrices can be generated by leverage score sampling with.   
10 generate independent random  $\delta_b^{(1)}$  and  $\delta_b^{(2)}$  such that; // lemma 51   
11 .  $\begin{array}{r}\mathbb{e}[\delta_b^{(1)}] = b - \mathbf{a}^\top x,\mathrm{and}\delta_b^{(1)}\approx \mathbb{e}[\delta_b^{(1)}] \end{array}$  where the approximation error should be as defined in lemma 51.   
12  $\delta_{\lambda}\gets h_{1}^{-1}h_{2}h_{3}^{-1}\delta_{b}^{(1)} + h_{4}\delta_{b}^{(2)}$  // this is computed and returned   
13  $\delta_x\leftarrow \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\delta_\lambda ,x^{(\mathrm{new})}\leftarrow x + \delta_x;\quad //$  defined for analysis and not computed   
14 return  $\delta_{\lambda}$

# b.2 increase of infeasibility due to  $x'$  and  $s'$

when  $x' \in \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n$  or  $s' \in \mathbb{r}_{\geq 0}^n$  changes by a multiplicative constant,  $\phi_b(x, x', s', \mu)$  changes by a multiplicative constant. as we want that  $x', s'$  are close to  $x, s$  and  $x, s$  change in every step of the ipm, this will blow up  $\phi_b$  too quickly.

our key idea is to move  $x$  to some nearby  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}$  as we replace  $x'$  and  $s'$  by  $x$  and  $s$ , such that (b.1) remains unchanged, i.e.  $\phi_b(x^{(\mathrm{new})}, x, s, \mu) \approx \phi_b(x, x', s', \mu)$ . algorithm 10 describes how we compute this  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}$  and we show in this subsection (lemma 53) that  $\phi_b$  does indeed not change by much when replacing  $x, x', s'$  by  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}, x, s$ .

to compute this new  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}$ , we need to estimate  $b - \mathbf{a}^{\top}x$ . the next lemma shows how to find

$\delta_{b}$  such that  $\mathbb{e}[\delta_b] = b - \mathbf{a}^\top x$  and  $\mu^{-1}\| \delta_b\|_{(\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{s}^{\prime -1}\mathbf{x}^\prime \mathbf{a})^{-1}}^2$  are small.

lemma 51 (generate  $\delta_b^{(1)}$  and  $\delta_b^{(2)})$ . let  $\rho \in (0,1 / 10)$  denote the failure probability, let  $\epsilon_{b}\in (0,1 / 10)$  denote an accuracy parameter. given  $\overline{\tau}\approx_1\tau (\overline{x},\overline{s})\in \mathbb{r}^n$  where  $x^{\prime}\approx_{1}x\approx_{1}\overline{x}$  and  $\overline{\mathbf{x}}\overline{s}\approx_{1}\mu \overline{\tau}$ . let  $\mathbf{q}' = \mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}'\mathbf{s}'^{-1}\mathbf{a}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$ . there is an algorithm that runs in  $\tilde{o}(d^2 /\epsilon_b)$  time and outputs vector  $\delta_b\in \mathbb{r}^d$  such that  $\mathbb{e}[\delta_b] = b - \mathbf{a}^\top x$ . furthermore, for any fixed vector  $v\in \mathbb{r}^d$  with  $\mu^{1 / 2}\| v\|_{\mathbf{q}'} = 1$ , we have

$$
\left| v ^ {\top} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} \right]\right) \right| \leq o \left(\sqrt {\epsilon_ {b}} \log \left(1 / \rho\right)\right) \tag {b.3}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \rho$ . furthermore, for any fixed matrix  $\mathbf{m} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$ , we have

$$
\mu^ {- 1 / 2} \| \mathbf {m} (\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {b} ]\right) \| _ {2} = \| \mathbf {m} \| _ {f} \cdot o \left(\sqrt {\epsilon_ {b}} \log (d / \rho)\right)
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \rho$ .

proof. we construct  $\delta_b \in \mathbb{r}^d$  by the following random sampling scheme:

$$
\delta_ {b} = b - \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} a _ {i} \cdot \widetilde {x} _ {i}
$$

where  $a_{i}$  is the  $i$ -th row of  $\mathbf{a}$  and

$$
\widetilde {x} _ {i} = \left\{ \begin{array}{l l} \frac {x _ {i}}{p _ {i}} & \text {w i t h p r o b a b i l i t y} p _ {i} = \min  \{1, \overline {{\tau}} _ {i} / \epsilon_ {b} \} \\ 0 & \text {o t h e r w i s e s} \end{array} \right..
$$

clearly, we have  $\mathbb{e}[\delta_b] = b - \mathbf{a}^\top x$ .

fix any vector  $v \in \mathbb{r}^d$  such that  $\mu^{1/2} \| v \|_{\mathbf{q}'} = 1$ . if  $p_i = 1$ , then  $\widetilde{x}_i = x_i$  and clearly  $|v^\top a_i \cdot (\widetilde{x}_i - x_i)| = 0 = o(\epsilon_b^2)$ . on the other hand, if  $p_i < 1$  then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \widetilde {x} _ {i} \right| ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{p _ {i} ^ {2}} \cdot \left(v ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} e _ {i}\right) ^ {2} \\ = \frac {1}{p _ {i} ^ {2}} \cdot \left(v ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} e _ {i}\right) ^ {2} \\ \leq \frac {1}{p _ {i} ^ {2}} \cdot \| v \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}} ^ {2} \cdot e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x a} (\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} e _ {i} \\ = \frac {x _ {i} ^ {2}}{p _ {i} ^ {2}} \cdot \frac {s _ {i} ^ {\prime}}{x _ {i} ^ {\prime}} \cdot \| v \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}} ^ {2} \cdot e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} e _ {i} \\ = \frac {x _ {i} ^ {2}}{p _ {i} ^ {2}} \cdot \frac {s _ {i} ^ {\prime}}{x _ {i} ^ {\prime}} \cdot \| v \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}} ^ {2} \cdot \sigma (\mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {a}) _ {i}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third step follows from cauchy-schwarz.

using that  $x^{\prime}\approx_{1}x$ $\mathbf{x}'s'\approx_{1}\mu \overline{\tau}$  , and  $\overline{\tau}\approx_{1}\tau (x^{\prime},s^{\prime})$  , we have

$$
\left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \widetilde {x} _ {i} \right| ^ {2} = o \left(\overline {{\tau}} _ {i} ^ {2} / p _ {i} ^ {2}\right) = o \left(\epsilon_ {b} ^ {2}\right).
$$

where the last step follows from  $p_i = \overline{\tau}_i / \epsilon_b$  (implied by  $p_i < 1$ ). further, as in this case, we have

$$
\left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot x _ {i} \right| ^ {2} \leq p _ {i} ^ {2} \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \widetilde {x} _ {i} \right| ^ {2} = o \left(\epsilon_ {b} ^ {2}\right).
$$

we see that in either case we have

$$
\left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \left(\widetilde {x} _ {i} - x _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} = o \left(\epsilon_ {b} ^ {2}\right). \tag {b.4}
$$

next, we note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \left(\widetilde {x} _ {i} - x _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} \right] = \sum_ {p _ {i} <   1} p _ {i} \cdot \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot \left(x _ {i} / p _ {i} - x _ {i}\right) \right| ^ {2} \\ \leq \sum_ {p _ {i} <   1} \frac {1}{p _ {i}} \cdot \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} x _ {i} \right| ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {p _ {i} <   1} \frac {1}{p _ {i}} \cdot (v ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} e _ {i}) ^ {2} \\ = \epsilon_ {b} \sum_ {p _ {i} <   1} \left(v ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \frac {e _ {i}}{\sqrt {\bar {\tau} _ {i}}}\right) ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

where the second step follows from  $p_i \in [0,1]$ , and the last step follows from  $\overline{\tau}_i = p_i\epsilon_b$  as we only consider  $p_i < 1$ . using  $x' \approx_1 x$  and  $\mathbf{x}'s' \approx_1 \mu \overline{\tau}$  this implies

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} \mathbb {e} \left[ \left| v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot (\widetilde {x} _ {i} - x _ {i}) \right| ^ {2} \right] = o (\epsilon_ {b}) \sum_ {p _ {i} <   1} (v ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \sqrt {\mu \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1}} e _ {i}) ^ {2} \\ = o (\epsilon_ {b}) \cdot \mu \cdot v ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a} v = o (\epsilon_ {b}). \qquad \mathrm {(b . 5)} \\ \end{array}
$$

using (b.4) and (b.5), bernstein inequality shows

$$
\left| v ^ {\top} (\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {b} ]) \right| = \left| \sum_ {i \in [ n ]} v ^ {\top} a _ {i} \cdot (\widetilde {x} _ {i} - x _ {i}) \right| = o (\sqrt {\epsilon_ {b} \log (1 / \rho)} + \epsilon_ {b} \log (1 / \rho)) = o (\sqrt {\epsilon_ {b}} \log (1 / \rho))
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \rho$ .

for the last conclusion, we consider the svd of  $\mathbf{m} \in \mathbb{r}^{d \times d}$  and let  $v_{i} \in \mathbb{r}^{d}$  such that  $v_{i}^{\top} \mathbf{m} = \lambda_{i} u_{i}^{\top} \in \mathbb{r}^{d}$  for some orthonormal  $v_{i}$  and  $u_{i} \in \mathbb{r}^{d}$ . note that  $\| (\mathbf{q}')^{-\frac{1}{2}} u_{i} \|_{\mathbf{q}'} = 1$  and (b.3) shows

$$
\left| \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot u _ {i} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {b} ]\right) \right| = o \left(\sqrt {\epsilon_ {b}} \log (d / \rho)\right) \tag {b.6}
$$

for all  $i$ . hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \mathbf {m} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {b} ]\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ d ]} \left(\mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot v _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {m} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} \right]\right)\right) ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ d ]} \lambda_ {i} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\mu^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot u _ {i} ^ {\top} \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} \right]\right)\right) ^ {2} \\ = \sum_ {i \in [ d ]} ^ {d} \lambda_ {i} ^ {2} \cdot o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho)) = \| \mathbf {m} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho)) \\ \end{array}
$$

where the third step follows from (b.6), and the last step follows from  $\sum_{i\in [d]}\lambda_i^2 = \| \mathbf{m}\| _f^2$

now, we show that  $\phi_b$  changes slowly if we move  $x$  to some nearby  $x^{(\mathrm{new})}$  when replacing  $x^{\prime}, s^{\prime}$  by  $x, s$ , according to the method maintaininfeasibility of algorithm 11.

lemma 52 (generate  $\mathbf{h}_2$  and  $\mathbf{h}_4$ ). for  $x \approx_{0.1} x'$  and  $s \approx_{0.1} s'$ , we can generate  $\mathbf{h}_2$  and  $\mathbf{h}_4$  with properties as defined in algorithm 10 in such a way that

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \leq o \left(\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}\right)
$$

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} ] \leq o (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}).
$$

with high probability, the time for computing  $\mathbf{h}_2b$  for any  $b\in \mathbb{r}^d$  is  $o(d^{2}\epsilon_{h}^{-2}\log n)$  and the time for computing  $\mathbf{h}_4b$  is  $o(t\log n + d^{2}\epsilon_{h}^{-2}\log^{2}n)$  where  $t$  is the time to compute  $\mathbf{m}b$  for  $\mathbf{m}$  satisfying  $\mathbb{e}\mathbf{m} = \mathbf{q}'^{-1}$  and  $\mathbf{m}\approx_{\epsilon_h / 4}\mathbf{q}'$ .

proof. the statement for  $\| \mathbf{q}^{\prime -1 / 2}\mathbf{h}_2\mathbf{q}^{\prime -1 / 2}\| _f^2$  follows directly from lemma 48 and lemma 49 when we perform the same leverage score sampling on both  $\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}$  and  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}(\mathbf{x}\mathbf{x}^{\prime})^{\frac{1}{2}}(\mathbf{s}\mathbf{s}^{\prime})^{-\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{a}$  (i.e. both matrices sample the same entries of their diagonal).

for the other statement, we let  $\mathbf{d} = \mathbf{x}'\mathbf{s}'^{-1}$  and  $\delta = \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1} - \mathbf{x}'\mathbf{s}'^{-1}$ . by the assumption, we have  $\max_i |\mathbf{d}_{ii}^{-1}\delta_{ii}| \leq \frac{1}{4}$ . by taylor expansion, we have

$$
\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\mathbf {d} + \delta) \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} - \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} = \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k \geq 1} \left(- \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a}\right) \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {k}.
$$

similar to lemma 42, we can use this to generate a spectral approximation with good expectation by truncating this series by some random  $x$  with  $\mathbb{p}[x\geq k] = 2^{-k}$  and scaling the terms with  $2^{k}$  so that the expectation stays the same. more precisely, we let  $\mathbf{m}^{(k)}$  be independent solvers satisfying  $\mathbf{m}^{(k)}\approx_{\epsilon_h / 4}(\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{d}\mathbf{a})^{-1}$  with  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{m}^{(k)}] = (\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{d}\mathbf{a})^{-1}$ , we let  $\mathbf{n}^{(k)}$  are i.i.d random leverage score samplings  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\delta^{(k)}\mathbf{a}$  of  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\delta \mathbf{a}$  such that

$$
\| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d a}) ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d a}) ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} \leq e ^ {\epsilon_ {h} / 4} \max _ {i} | \mathbf {d} _ {i i} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {i i} |
$$

and  $\mathbb{e}[\mathbf{n}^{(k)}] = \mathbf{a}^\top \delta \mathbf{a}$ . now, we define

$$
\mathbf {h} _ {4} \stackrel {\mathrm {d e f}} {=} \mathbf {m} ^ {(0)} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {x} (- 2) ^ {k} \prod_ {i = 1} ^ {k} (\mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}).
$$

since  $\mathbf{m}^{(i)}$ ,  $\mathbf{n}^{(i)}$  are independent, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {x, \mathbf {m}, \mathbf {n}} [ \mathbf {h} _ {4} ] = (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d a}) ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} _ {x} \left[ \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {x} 2 ^ {k} (- (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a}) (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d a}) ^ {- 1}) ^ {k} \right] \\ = (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} \mathbb {p} [ x \geq k ] 2 ^ {k} (- (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a}) (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}) ^ {k} \\ = \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} \left(- \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta \mathbf {a}\right) \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) ^ {k} \\ = (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\mathbf {d} + \delta) \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} - (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to bound the frobenius norm, we let  $\mathbf{y} \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} (\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{d}\mathbf{a})^{\frac{1}{2}}$  and note that

$$
\| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \leq \left(\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {x} (\frac {2}{3}) ^ {k}\right) \cdot \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {x} (\frac {3}{2}) ^ {k} \| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} ^ {(0)} (- 2) ^ {k} \prod_ {i = 1} ^ {k} (\mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2}
$$

where we used that  $\| \sum_{i = 1}^{x}\mathbf{a}_{i}\|_{f}^{2}\leq (\sum_{i = 1}^{x}\| \mathbf{a}_{i}\|_{f})^{2}\leq (\sum_{k = 1}^{x}(\frac{2}{3})^{k})\cdot (\sum_{i = 1}^{x}(\frac{3}{2})^{k}\| \mathbf{a}_{i}\|_{f}^{2})$

taking expectation, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {x, \mathbf {m}, \mathbf {n}} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \leq \left(\sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} (\frac {2}{3}) ^ {k}\right) \cdot \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} 3 ^ {k} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {m}, \mathbf {n}} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} ^ {(0)} \prod_ {i = 1} ^ {k} (\mathbf {n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)}) \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \\ \leq 3 \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} 3 ^ {k} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {m}, \mathbf {n}} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} ^ {(0)} \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \prod_ {i = 1} ^ {k} (\mathbf {y n} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {y y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {m} ^ {(i)} \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1}) \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

using that  $\| \mathbf{y}^{-1}\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\mathbf{y}^{-1}\| _2\leq e^{\epsilon_h / 4}$  and  $\| \mathbf{y}\mathbf{n}^{(i)}\mathbf{y}\| _2\leq e^{\epsilon_h / 4}\max_i|\mathbf{d}_{ii}^{-1}\delta_{ii}|\leq e^{\epsilon_h / 4}$  as  $x\approx_{0.1}x^{\prime}$  and  $s\approx_{0.1}s^{\prime}$ . using  $\epsilon_h\leq \frac{1}{10}$ , we have that  $\| \mathbf{y}^{-1}\mathbf{m}^{(i)}\mathbf{y}^{-1}\| _2\leq e^{1 / 40}$  and  $\| \mathbf{y}\mathbf{n}^{(i)}\mathbf{y}\| _2\leq e^{1 / 40}\cdot \frac{1}{4}$ . hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {x, \mathbf {m}, \mathbf {n}} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {y} ^ {- 1} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \leq 3 \sum_ {k = 1} ^ {\infty} 3 ^ {k} e ^ {1 / 4 0 (2 k - 2)} \frac {1}{4 ^ {k - 1}} \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {y n} ^ {(1)} \mathbf {y} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \\ = o (1) \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} \mathbf {n} ^ {(1)} \mathbf {y} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \\ \end{array}
$$

finally, lemma 48 and lemma 49 shows that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {y} \mathbf {n} ^ {(1)} \mathbf {y} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] = \mathbb {e} \left[ \| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {n} ^ {(1)} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {d} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \operatorname {d i a g} \left(\mathbf {d} ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \delta \mathbf {d} ^ {- \frac {1}{2}}\right) \| _ {\sigma (\mathbf {d} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a})} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot o (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}) \\ \end{array}
$$

lemma 53 (infeasibility change due to norm change). assume that  $\mathbf{x}s\approx_{1}\mu \tau$ ,  $x^{\prime}\approx_{0.1}x$ ,  $s^{\prime}\approx_{0.1}s\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$ . consider  $x^{(\mathrm{new})} = x + \delta_x\in \mathbb{r}^n$  defined in algorithm 10. then, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\delta_ {x}} [ \phi_ {b} (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}, x, s, \mu) ] = (1 + o (\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2})) \cdot \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) \\ + o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \cdot (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}). \\ \end{array}
$$

furthermore,

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})} - x) \| _ {\overline {{\tau}}} ^ {2} \leq o \big (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho) + \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) \big) \cdot \big (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} \big) \\ + o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} d \log^ {2} (d / \rho)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})} - x) \| _ {\infty} ^ {2} \leq o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho) + \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu)) \cdot (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}) \\ + o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} d \log^ {2} (d / \rho)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

with probability at least  $1 - \rho$ .

proof. we define the following three matrices

$$
\delta_ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} = (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} - (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {d \times d},
$$

$$
\delta_ {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2}} = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n \times n},
$$

$$
\delta_ {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n \times n}.
$$

and the following two matrices

$$
\mathbf {q} = \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \text {a n d} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime} = \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}.
$$

let  $\delta_{\lambda} \in \mathbb{r}^{d}$  be defined as algorithm 10. then, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {\lambda} ] = \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} + \mathbf {h} _ {4} \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} ] \\ = \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \right] \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {2} \right] \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \right] \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} \right] + \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {4} \right] \cdot \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} \right] \\ = \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} - \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2}\right) \mathbf {a} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x) + \left(\mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1}\right) (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x) \\ = \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \delta_ {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2}} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x) + \delta_ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x) \\ = - \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \delta_ {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b). \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} \left[ x ^ {\text {(n e w)}} \right] - b\right) \\ = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) + \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a e} [ \delta_ {\lambda} ] \\ = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) - \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \delta_ {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \\ = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \\ - \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \\ + \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {\prime - 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \\ = \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {a q} ^ {\prime - 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \tag {b.7} \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \phi_ {b} (\mathbb {e} [ x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}} ], x, s, \mu) \\ = \| \mathbf {x} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {s} ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbb {e} [ x ^ {(\text {n e w})} ] - b) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {x} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {s} ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {s} ^ {- \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {a} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x - b\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = \phi_ {b} \left(x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from definition of  $\phi_b$  (see (b.1)), the second step follows from (b.7), the third step follows from property of a projection matrix, the last step follows from definition of  $\phi_b$  (see (b.1)).

hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \phi_ {b} (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, x, s, \mu) = \phi_ {b} (\mathbb {e} [ x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}} ], x, s, \mu) + \mu^ {- 1} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}} - \mathbb {e} [ x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}} ]) \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ \leq \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) + \mu^ {- 1} \| \delta_ {\lambda} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {\lambda} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \delta_ {\lambda} - \mathbb {e} [ \delta_ {\lambda} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \right] \leq \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \right] \\ + \mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {h} _ {4} \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} - \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {4} \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \right]. \tag {b.8} \\ \end{array}
$$
