for the first term in (b.8), and  $\delta_{b} = b - \mathbf{a}^{\top}x$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} \right] \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ \leq \left\| \left(\mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {2} ] \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1}\right) \delta_ {b} \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ + \left\| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \left(\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}\right) \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ \leq \left\| \left(\mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1}\right) \delta_ {b} \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ + \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {h} _ {2} - \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {2} ]\right) \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1} \delta_ {b} \right\| \mathbf {q} \\ + \left\| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} (\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}) \right\| \mathbf {q} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \left(\mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1}\right) \delta_ {b} \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \tag {b.9} \\ + \left\| \left(\mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} - \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1}\right) \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1} \delta_ {b} \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ + \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {- 1} \left(\mathbf {h} _ {2} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \mathbf {h} _ {2} \right]\right) \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1} \delta_ {b} \right\| _ {\mathbf {q}} \\ + \left\| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} (\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}) \right\| \mathbf {q} \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\mathbb{e}_{\mathbf{h}_1}[\mathbf{h}_1^{-1}] = \mathbf{q}^{-1}$ ,  $\mathbb{e}_{\mathbf{h}_3}[\mathbf{h}_3^{-1}] = \mathbf{q}'^{-1}$  and  $\mathbb{e}_{\delta_b^{(1)}}[\delta_b^{(1)}] = \delta_b$ . note that  $-\mathbf{q}' \preceq \mathbb{e}_{\mathbf{h}_2}[\mathbf{h}_2] \preceq \mathbf{q}'$ ,  $\mathbf{h}_1^{-1} \approx_{\epsilon_h} \mathbf{q}^{-1}$ ,  $\mathbf{h}_2 = \mathbb{e}_{\mathbf{h}_2}[\mathbf{h}_2] \pm \epsilon_h \cdot \mathbf{q}'$ ,  $\mathbf{h}_3^{-1} \approx_{\epsilon_h} \mathbf{q}^{-1}$ , and  $\mathbf{q} \approx_1 \mathbf{q}'$  so the first three terms of (b.9) are all upper bounded by

$$
o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \| \delta_ {b} \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1}} ^ {2}\right) = \mu \cdot o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu\right)\right).
$$

for the last term of (b.9) note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} (\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}) \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \leq 2 \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} (\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}) \| ^ {2} \\ = 2 \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \left(\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \delta_ {b}\right) \| ^ {2} \\ = \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \\ = \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \\ \leq \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used lemma 51. in summary this means the first term in (b.8) can be bounded via

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1}, \mathbf {h} _ {2}, \mathbf {h} _ {3}, \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)}} \left[ \| \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {1} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \right] \leq o (\mu \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \cdot \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu)) \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {2}} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \cdot o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d). \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {4}, \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)}} \left[ \| \mathbf {h} _ {4} \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} - \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {4}, \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)}} [ \mathbf {h} _ {4} \delta_ {b} ^ {(2)} ] \| _ {\mathbf {q}} ^ {2} \right] \leq o (\mu \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu)) \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {4}} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \cdot o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \\ \end{array}
$$

which via (b.8) leads to

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} [ \phi_ {b} (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, x, s, \mu) ] = (1 + o (\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2})) \cdot \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {2}} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \cdot o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \\ + \mathbb {e} _ {\mathbf {h} _ {4}} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {h} _ {4} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \right] \cdot o (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d) \\ = (1 + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right)) \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu\right) \\ + o ((\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}) \epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used lemma 52.

for the movement of the step, note that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \left(x ^ {\text {(n e w)}} - x\right) \right\| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} = \left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \delta_ {\lambda} \right\| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} = o \left(\mu^ {- 1}\right) \cdot \left\| \delta_ {\lambda} \right\| _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}} ^ {2} \tag {b.10}
$$

where we used that  $\mathbf{x}s\approx_{1}\mu \tau (x,s),\overline{x}^{\prime}\approx_{1}\overline{x}\approx_{1}x\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$ . let  $\delta_{\lambda}^{(1)} = \mathbf{h}_1^{-1}\mathbf{h}_2\mathbf{h}_3^{-1}\delta_b^{(1)}\in \mathbb{r}^d$ . using lemma 51, we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \delta_ {\lambda} ^ {(1)} \| _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}} ^ {2} = o (1) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \left(\delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} - \mathbb {e} \left[ \delta_ {b} ^ {(1)} \right]\right) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ + o (1) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o (\mu) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \cdot \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho)\right) \\ + o (\mu) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu) \\ \end{array}
$$

with probability  $1 - \rho$ . using the definition of  $\mathbf{h}_2$  and lemma 52, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} = \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot \left(\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {2} ] \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} (\mathbf {h} _ {2} - \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {2} ]) \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} ^ {2}\right) \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbb {e} [ \mathbf {h} _ {2} ] \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} + o (\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}) \\ \leq o \left(\mathbb {e} \left[ \| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \right] + \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) \\ = o \big (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \big) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\| \mathbf{q}'^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathbf{h}_3^{-1}\mathbf{q}'^{\frac{1}{2}}\| _2^2 = o(1),\mathbb{e}[x^2 ]\geq \mathbb{e}[x]^2,\| \mathbf{a}\| _2^2\leq \| \mathbf{a}\| _f^2$  , and lemma (52).

similarly, we have

$$
\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime - \frac {1}{2}} \mathbf {h} _ {2} \mathbf {h} _ {3} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime \frac {1}{2}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \leq o (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + d \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}).
$$

hence, we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \delta_ {\lambda} ^ {(1)} \| _ {\mathbf {q} ^ {\prime}} ^ {2} = o (\mu (\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho) + \phi_ {b} (x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu))) \cdot (\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau (x, s)} ^ {2}). \\ + o (\mu \epsilon_ {b} \epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} d \log^ {2} (d / \rho)) \\ \end{array}
$$

similarly, we have the same bound for  $\| \delta_{\lambda}^{(2)}\|_{\mathbf{q}'}^2$ . putting these two into (b.10) gives the result.

finally for the  $\ell_{\infty}$ -norm we have

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\infty} = \max  _ {i \in [ n ]} | e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} |
$$

where for any  $i\in [n]$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right| ^ {2} = \left| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \delta_ {\lambda} \right| ^ {2} \\ = \left| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a q} ^ {\prime - 1 / 2} \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \delta_ {\lambda} \right| ^ {2} \\ \leq \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \mathbf {a s e} _ {i} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \left\| \mathbf {q} ^ {\prime 1 / 2} \delta_ {\lambda} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \\ = o (1) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} (\mathbf {x s}) ^ {- 1 / 2} e _ {i} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \delta_ {\lambda} \| _ {\mathbf {a x} ^ {\prime} \mathbf {s} ^ {\prime - 1} \mathbf {a}} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\left(\mu \tau (x, s) _ {i}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) \cdot \| \mathbf {q} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {a x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} e _ {i} \| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot \| \delta_ {\lambda} \| _ {\mathbf {a} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\left(\mu \tau (x, s) _ {i}\right) ^ {- 1}\right) \cdot \sigma (x, s) _ {i} \cdot \| \delta_ {\lambda} \| _ {\mathbf {a} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}} ^ {2} \\ = o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \epsilon_ {h} d \log^ {2} (d / \rho) + \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d / \rho) + \phi_ {b} \left(x, x ^ {\prime}, s ^ {\prime}, \mu\right)\right)\right) \cdot \left(\| \ln x ^ {\prime} - \ln x \| _ {\tau} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {\prime} - \ln s \| _ {\tau} ^ {2}\right). \\ \end{array}
$$

algorithm 11: maintaining feasibility  
1 procedure maintainfeasibility  $(x\in \mathbb{r}^n,s\in \mathbb{r}^n,\overline{\tau}\in \mathbb{r}^n)$    
2  $\delta_{\lambda}\gets$  maintainineasibility  $(x,s,\overline{\tau})$  ; // make  $\phi_b(x + \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\delta_\lambda ,x,s,\mu)$  unbiased, so it stays small with constant probability between any two execution of the following if-branch:   
3 if it has been  $\sqrt{d} /\log^{6}n$  iterations since this branch was executed then / reduce  $\phi_{b}$ $\delta_{\lambda} = \delta_{\lambda} + \mathbf{h}^{-1}(b - \mathbf{a}^{\top}x)\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  with  $\mathbf{h}\approx_{\epsilon_h}\mathbf{q}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d} / /\mathbf{q} = \mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}$  /\* with constant probability  $\phi_b(x + \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\delta_\lambda ,x,s,\mu)$  stays small. if it failed, re-try the past  $\tilde{\mathrm{o}} (\sqrt{d})$  iterations of the ipm since the last time this branch was executed.  $^ { \text{丰} }$    
5 return  $\delta_{\lambda}$

where the first step follows from definition of  $\delta_{x}$ , the fifth step follows from  $xs\approx \mu \tau (x,s)$ , the sixth step follows from the definition of leverage scores, and the last step follows from our previously proven bound on  $\| \delta_{\lambda}\|_{\mathbf{a}\overline{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}}$  and lemma 19 to bound  $\sigma (x,s)\leq \tau (x,s)$ .

# b.3 improving infeasibility

from section b.1 and b.2, we see that  $\phi_b$  increases slowly over time. here we show in lemma 54, that over  $\sqrt{d} / \log^6 n$  iterations of the ipm, the potential  $\phi_b$  changes only by a constant factor. intuitively, this can be seen by the ipm calling maintainfeasibility (algorithm 11) which in turn calls algorithm 10. the previous subsection showed that each call to algorithm 10 increases  $\phi_b$  by only a small amount. as seen in line 4 of algorithm 11, after  $\sqrt{d} / \log^6 n$  iterations we decrease the potential again. lemma 55 shows that line 4 does indeed decrease the potential  $\phi_b$  sufficiently.

lemma 54. consider  $t \leq \sqrt{d} / \log^6 n$  iterations of the algorithm 1 and let  $x^{(k)}, s^{(k)}$  be the input to the  $k$ -th call to algorithm 10. suppose that  $\phi_b(x^{(1)}, x^{(1)}, s^{(1)}, \mu^{(1)}) \leq \frac{\zeta\epsilon^2}{\log^6 n}$ ,  $\mu^{(k+1)} = (1 - \frac{\epsilon_\mu}{\sqrt{d}})\mu^{(k)}, \forall k \in [t]$  and  $\epsilon_b \leq \frac{c\zeta\epsilon^2}{\sqrt{d\log^2 n}}$ ,  $\epsilon_h \leq \frac{c\zeta\epsilon}{d^{1/4}}$  for some small enough constants  $\zeta, c > 0$ , where  $\epsilon_b$  is the accuracy parameter used in lemma 51. suppose that we update  $x$  using an unbiased linear system solver with accuracy  $\epsilon_h$  as defined in lemma 50 during the algorithm 1. assume further

$$
\| \ln x ^ {(k)} - \ln x ^ {(k - 1)} \| _ {\tau (x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}) + \infty} + \| \ln s ^ {(k)} - \ln s ^ {(k - 1)} \| _ {\tau (x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}) + \infty} \leq 0. 1 \text {f o r a l l} 1 <   k \leq t.
$$

then, we have

$$
\phi_ {b} (x ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)}, \mu^ {(k)}) \leq \frac {5 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} f o r a l l 1 <   k \leq t
$$

with probability at least  $\frac{1}{2}$ .

proof. let  $\delta_x^{(k)}$  be the vector  $\delta_x$  as defined in algorithm 10, when we currently perform the  $k$ -th call to that function. then we have that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \mathbb {e} \left[ \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k + 1)}, x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}, \mu^ {(k + 1)}\right) \right] \\ \leq \phi_ {b} (x ^ {(k)} + \delta_ {x} ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}, \mu^ {(k + 1)}) + o (\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}) \cdot \| h \| _ {\tau (x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)})} ^ {2} \\ \leq \left(1 + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right)\right) \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)}, \mu^ {(k + 1)}\right) + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \| h \| _ {\tau \left(x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}\right)} ^ {2} \\ + o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d\right) \cdot \left(\| \ln x ^ {(k)} - \ln x ^ {(k - 1)} \| _ {\tau \left(x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}\right)} + \| \ln s ^ {(k)} - \ln s ^ {(k - 1)} \| _ {\tau \left(x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}\right)}\right) \\ = \left(1 + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right)\right) \cdot \left(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\mu} d ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)}, \mu^ {(k)}\right) + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) \cdot \| h \| _ {\tau \left(x ^ {(k)}, s ^ {(k)}\right)} ^ {2} \\ + o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d\right) \\ = \left(1 + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right)\right) \cdot \left(1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\mu} d ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)}, \mu^ {(k)}\right) + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2}\right) + o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} d\right) \\ = (1 + 2 \epsilon_ {\mu} d ^ {- 1 / 2} + o \left(c \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}\right)) \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k)}, x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)}, \mu^ {(k)}\right) + o \left(c \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from lemma 50, the second step follows from lemma 53, the third step follows from  $\mu^{(k + 1)}\leq (1 - \epsilon_{\mu}d^{-1 / 2}\mu^{(k)})$  and  $\| \ln x^{(k)} - \ln x^{(k - 1)}\|_{\tau (x^{(k)},s^{(k)})} + \| \ln s^{(k)} - \ln s^{(k - 1)}\|_{\tau (x^{(k)},s^{(k)})}\leq 0.1$ , the fourth step follows from the step of the ipm with  $\| h\|_{\tau}^{2} = o(1)$ .

let  $\phi^{(k)} = \phi_b(x^{(k)},x^{(k - 1)},s^{(k - 1)},\mu^{(k)})$  . let

$$
\psi^ {(k)} = \frac {\phi^ {(k)} + c \zeta \epsilon^ {2} \log^ {- 6} n}{\frac {8 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} + k \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}}. \tag {b.11}
$$

note that  $\psi^{(k)}\geq 1 / 9$  for  $k\leq \sqrt{d} /\log^6 n$  (since  $\phi^{(k)}\geq 0$ ) and further there exists some  $c' = o(c)$  such that

$$
\mathbb {e} [ \psi^ {(k + 1)} ] \leq \frac {\phi^ {(k)} + c \zeta \epsilon^ {2} \log^ {- 6} n + c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta d ^ {- 1 / 2} + (2 \epsilon_ {\mu} + c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta) d ^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \phi^ {(k)}}{\frac {8 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} + k \epsilon^ {2} \zeta d ^ {- 1 / 2} + \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}}.
$$

we want to construct a supermartingale, so we want that this expectation is less than  $\psi^{(k)}$ . this is the case if

$$
\frac {c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta d ^ {- 1 / 2} + (2 \epsilon_ {\mu} + c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta) d ^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \phi^ {(k)}}{\zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}} \leq \frac {\phi^ {(k)} + c \zeta \epsilon^ {2} \log^ {- 6} n}{\frac {8 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} + k \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}} = \psi^ {(k)},
$$

so let us analyze which other conditions are required to satisfy this inequality. for now assume that  $\psi^{(k)}\leq 1$ , and  $k\leq \frac{\sqrt{d}}{\log^6n}$ , then

$$
\phi^ {(k)} \leq \frac {8 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} + k \zeta \epsilon d ^ {- 1 / 2} <   \frac {9 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n}.
$$

so if we choose  $c$  small enough such that  $2\epsilon_{\mu} + c'\epsilon^{2}\zeta \leq 1/100$  (note that  $\epsilon_{\mu} \ll 1/16000$  by theorem 5) and  $\phi^{(k)} \leq \frac{9\zeta\epsilon^{2}}{\log^{6}n}$ , then

$$
\frac {c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta d ^ {- 1 / 2} + (2 \epsilon_ {\mu} + c ^ {\prime} \epsilon^ {2} \zeta) d ^ {- 1 / 2} \cdot \phi^ {(k)}}{\zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}} \leq \frac {\epsilon^ {2} \zeta d ^ {- 1 / 2} / 1 0 0 + d ^ {- 1 / 2} \phi^ {(k)} / 1 0 0}{\zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}} \leq 1 / 9 \leq \psi^ {(k)},
$$

which in turn shows that  $\mathbb{e}[\psi^{(k + 1)}]\leq \psi^{(k)}$ . in general, without the assumption  $\psi^{(k)}\leq 1$ , we have

$$
\mathbb {e} \left[ \min  \left\{\psi^ {(k + 1)}, 1 \right\} \right] \leq \min  \left\{\psi^ {(k)}, 1 \right\}.
$$

hence, for  $k \leq \sqrt{d} / \log^6 n = t$  we have that  $\min \left\{\psi^{(k)}, 1\right\}$  is a non-negative supermartingale.

by ville's maximal inequality [vil39] for supermartingales, we have that

$$
\mathbb {p} \left[ \max _ {k \in [ t ]} \min \left\{\psi^ {(k)}, 1 \right\} > \frac {1}{2} \right] \leq \frac {\min \left\{\psi^ {(1)} , 1 \right\}}{\frac {1}{2}} \leq \frac {\frac {1}{4}}{\frac {1}{2}} = \frac {1}{2}.
$$

where the second step follows from  $\phi^{(1)}\leq \frac{1}{4}$ . hence,  $\max_{k\in [t]}\psi^{(k)}\leq \frac{1}{2}$  with probability at least  $\frac{1}{2}$ . under this event, we have for small enough  $c$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \max  _ {k \in [ t ]} \phi^ {(k)} \leq \max  _ {k \in [ t ]} \psi^ {(k)} \left(\frac {8 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} + k \zeta \epsilon^ {2} d ^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \\ \leq \frac {1}{2} \cdot \frac {9 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n} \\ <   \frac {5 \zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\log^ {6} n}. \\ \end{array}
$$

![](images/e5c1092dee7e7a4664e450281cc2019178fc27278f905f611ae0cb30a56c8010.jpg)

now, to move  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}x - b$  closer to 0, we solve the equation

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {x}}} \delta_ {s} + \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} \delta_ {x} = 0, \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n}
$$

$$
\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \delta_ {x} = \delta_ {b}, \in \mathbb {r} ^ {d}
$$

$$
\mathbf {a} \delta_ {y} + \delta_ {s} = 0 \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n}
$$

with  $\delta_{b} = b - \mathbf{a}^{\top}x$ . this gives the formula

$$
\delta_ {s} = - \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b},
$$

$$
\delta_ {x} = \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \delta_ {b}.
$$

we see in algorithm 11 that every  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(\sqrt{d})$  iterations we will try to decrease the potential  $\phi_b$ . the following lemma shows that such a step decreases  $\phi_b$  sufficiently.

lemma 55. let  $\widehat{x},x,s\in \mathbb{r}_{>0}^n$  and consider  $x^{(\mathrm{new})} = \widehat{x} +\delta_x\in \mathbb{r}^n$  where  $\delta_{x} = \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\mathbf{h}^{-1}(b-\mathbf{a}^{\top}\widehat{x})\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  with  $\mathbf{h}\approx_{\epsilon_h}\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  for  $\epsilon_h\in (0,1 / 20]$ . then, we have that

$$
\phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {\text {(n e w)}}, x, s, \mu\right) \leq 5 \epsilon_ {h} \cdot \phi_ {b} \left(\widehat {x}, x, s, \mu\right).
$$

proof. for any  $p > 0$ , we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \phi_ {b} (x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}}, x, s, \mu) = \mu^ {- 1} \cdot \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x ^ {\mathrm {(n e w)}} - b \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = \mu^ {- 1} \cdot \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} + \delta_ {x}) - b \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = \mu^ {- 1} \cdot \| (\mathbf {i} - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}) (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b) \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2}, \\ \end{array}
$$

where the first step follows from definition of  $\phi_b$  (see (b.1)), the second step follows from  $x^{(\mathrm{new})} = \widehat{x} +\delta_x$ , and the last step follows from definition of  $\delta_{x}$ .

using  $\mathbf{h}\approx_{\epsilon_h}\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} (\mathbf {i} - \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} (\mathbf {i} - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1}) \\ = \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1} - 2 \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} + \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} \\ \preceq (1 - 2 e ^ {- \epsilon_ {h}} + e ^ {2 \epsilon_ {h}}) \cdot (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \\ \preceq 5 \epsilon_ {h} \cdot (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}. \\ \end{array}
$$

where the last step follows from  $e^{2\epsilon_h} \leq 1 + 3\epsilon_h$  and  $e^{-\epsilon_h} \geq 1 - \epsilon_h$  for  $\epsilon_h \in (0, 1/20]$ .

hence, we have

$$
\phi_ {b} (x ^ {(\mathrm {n e w})}, x, s, \mu) \leq 5 \epsilon_ {h} \mu^ {- 1} \cdot \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b \| _ {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} = 5 \epsilon_ {h} \cdot \phi_ {b} (\widehat {x}, x, s, \mu).
$$

![](images/03f946fd41af0a1e5a01d063e76ca767104724bc77e6632b8376bf690a8fbafd.jpg)

here we show that the correction step of lemma 55 does not change the solution  $x$  by much, provided that  $\phi_b$  is small.

lemma 56. let  $\widehat{x},x,s\in \mathbb{r}_{>0}^n$  and consider  $\delta_{x} = \mathbf{x}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{a}\mathbf{h}^{-1}(b - \mathbf{a}^{\top}\widehat{x})\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  with  $\mathbf{h}\approx_{\epsilon_h}$ $\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{s}^{-1}\mathbf{x}\mathbf{a}\in \mathbb{r}^{d\times d}$  with  $\epsilon_h\in (0,1 / 20]$  as in lemma 55. then, we have that

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \right\| _ {\tau (x, s)} \leq o (1) \cdot \phi_ {b} (\widehat {x}, x, s, \mu) ^ {1 / 2}
$$

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\infty} \leq o (1) \cdot \phi_ {b} (\widehat {x}, x, s, \mu) ^ {1 / 2}
$$

proof. for the bound on  $\| \mathbf{x}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}$  consider the following

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\infty} = o (1) \cdot \max _ {i \in [ n ]} \| 1 _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x}) \| _ {\infty} \\ \leq o (1) \cdot \max _ {i \in [ n ]} \| 1 _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x}) \| _ {2} \\ \leq o (1) \cdot \max _ {i \in [ n ]} \| 1 _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} \| \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \hat {x}) \| _ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

here the first factor can be bounded via

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| e _ {i} ^ {\top} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \| _ {2} ^ {2} = o (1) \cdot 1 _ {i} ^ {\top} \frac {1}{(\mathbf {x s}) ^ {1 / 2}} \frac {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2}}{\mathbf {s} ^ {1 / 2}} \mathbf {a} (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}) ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \frac {\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2}}{\mathbf {s} ^ {1 / 2}} \frac {1}{(\mathbf {x s} ^ {\prime})} 1 _ {i} \\ = o (1) \cdot \frac {\sigma (\mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a}) _ {i}}{x _ {i} s _ {i}} = o (\mu^ {- 1}), \\ \end{array}
$$

where at the end we used  $\sigma (\mathbf{x}^{1 / 2}\mathbf{s}^{-1 / 2}\mathbf{a})\leq \sigma (\mathbf{x}^{1 / 2 - \alpha}\mathbf{s}^{-1 / 2 - \alpha}\mathbf{a})\leq \tau (x,s)$  via lemma 19 and  $xs\approx \mu \tau$  . the last term can be bounded by

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1 / 2} \left(b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x}\right) \right\| _ {2} = o (1) \cdot \left\| b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} \right\| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} \\ = o (\mu^ {1 / 2}) \cdot \sqrt {\phi_ {b} (\widehat {x} , x , s , \mu)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

thus in summary we have  $\| \mathbf{x}^{-1}\delta_x\|_{\infty}\leq o(1)\cdot \sqrt{\phi_b(\hat{x},x,s,\mu)}$ . for the  $\| \cdot \|_{\tau}$  norm we have because of  $xs = \mu \tau$ , and the definition of  $\phi_b$  that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {\tau (x, s)} \leq o (\mu^ {- 1 / 2}) \cdot \| (\mathbf {x s}) ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} \delta_ {x} \| _ {2} \\ \leq o (\mu^ {- 1 / 2}) \cdot \| \mathbf {x} ^ {1 / 2} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1 / 2} \mathbf {a} \mathbf {h} ^ {- 1} (b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \hat {x}) \| _ {2} \\ = o \left(\mu^ {- 1 / 2}\right) \cdot \| b - \mathbf {a} \widehat {x} \| _ {\left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \mathbf {x} \mathbf {s} ^ {- 1} \mathbf {a}\right) ^ {- 1}} \\ = o (1) \cdot \phi_ {b} (\widehat {x}, x, s, \mu) ^ {1 / 2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

to obtain the final solution of our lp,  $\phi_b = \omega(1 / \log^6 n)$  is still too large. here we show that iterative application of lemma 55 yields a very accurate solution.

corollary 57. there exists some small enough  $\zeta = o(1)$ , such that given a primal dual pair  $(x,s)$  with  $\phi_b(x,x,s,\mu) \leq \frac{4\zeta\epsilon}{\log n}$  and  $xs \approx_{1/4} \mu \tau(x,s)$  and any  $\delta > 0$ , we can compute an  $x'$  with

$$
\phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {\prime}, x ^ {\prime}, s, \mu\right) \leq \delta ,
$$

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} (x - x ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\tau (x, s) + \infty} \leq o \left(\phi_ {b} ^ {1 / 2} (x, x, s, \mu)\right),
$$

$$
x ^ {\prime} s \approx_ {1 / 2} \mu \tau (x ^ {\prime}, s)
$$

in  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}} ((nd + d^3)\log (1 / \delta))$  time.

proof. this follows by repeatedly applying lemma 55 for small enough  $\epsilon_h = o(1)$ . we need  $o(\log \delta^{-1})$  repetitions to decrease  $\phi_b(x', x, s, \mu)$  down to  $c\delta$  for some small enough  $c = o(1)$ . note that by lemma 56 the total movement is bounded by  $o\left(\phi_b^{1/2}(x, x, s, \mu)\right) = o(1)$  as the movement per iteration is exponentially decaying. at last, going from  $\phi_b^{1/2}(x', x, s, \mu)$  to  $\phi_b^{1/2}(x', x', s, \mu)$  increases the potential by at most some  $o(1)$  factor given that  $x$  and  $x'$  differ by at most a constant factor. thus for small enough  $c = o(1)$  we have  $\phi_b^{1/2}(x', x, s, \mu) \leq \delta$ . likewise we have  $x's \approx_{1/2} \mu\tau(x', s)$  when the constant factor difference between  $x$  and  $x'$  is small enough which can be guaranteed by choosing small enough  $\zeta > 0$ .

throughout the ipm we move  $x$  several times. first, we perform the classic ipm step and then we perform the corrective steps of algorithm 10 and 11. note that by lemma 53 the extra movement of  $x$  depends on how much  $x$  moved in the previous iteration. here we show that this does not create an amplifying feedback loop, i.e. as long as  $\phi_b \leq 5\zeta \epsilon^2 / \log^6 n$ , the total movement  $\| \mathbf{x}^{-1}(x - x') \|_{\tau + \infty}$  is always bounded by  $\frac{\epsilon}{2}$ . by induction over the number of iterations lemma 53 and lemma 58 then imply that we always have  $\phi_b \leq 5\zeta \epsilon^2 / \log^6 n$  and  $\| \mathbf{x}^{-1}(x - x') \|_{\tau + \infty} \leq \epsilon / 2$ .

lemma 58. for some small enough constants  $\zeta, c > 0$  let  $\epsilon_b \leq \frac{c\zeta\epsilon^2}{\sqrt{d\log^2n}}$ ,  $\epsilon_h \leq \frac{c\zeta\epsilon}{d^{1/4}\log^3n}$  in maintainfeasibility and let  $\epsilon$  be the parameter of theorem 5. let  $x^{(k)}, s^{(k)}$  the inputs of the  $k$ -th call to maintainfeasibility. assume

$$
\phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k - 1)}, x ^ {(k - 2)}, s ^ {(k - 2)}, \mu^ {(k - 1)}\right) \leq 5 \zeta \epsilon^ {2} / \log^ {6} n
$$

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {(k - 2) - 1} (x ^ {(k - 1)} - x ^ {(k - 2)}) \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {\epsilon}{2}
$$

for all  $k' < k$ , then we have with high probability

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {(k) - 1} \left(x ^ {(k)} - x ^ {(k - 1)}\right) \right\| _ {\infty + \tau} ^ {2} \leq \epsilon^ {2} / 2. \tag {b.12}
$$

further let  $e_x \in \mathbb{r}^n$  be the movement of  $x$  induced by calling maintainfeasibility (algorithm 11), then for small enough constant  $\zeta > 0$  we have with high probability that

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} e _ {x} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {\gamma \alpha}{2 ^ {2 0}}
$$

proof. theorem 32 yields the bound (b.12) if the extra movement  $e_x$  caused by maintainfeasibility satisfies  $\| \mathbf{x}^{-1}e_x\|_{\tau +\infty}\leq \frac{\gamma\alpha}{220}$ .

we define three errors: 1. the movement due to maintaininfeasibility, say  $\mathrm{err}_{\mathrm{min}}$ ; 2, the movement due to if-condition, say  $\mathrm{err}_{\mathrm{ifc}}$ . then we know that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname {e r r} _ {\operatorname {m i n}} ^ {2} = o \left(\epsilon_ {b} \log^ {2} (d) + \phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k - 1)}, x ^ {(k - 2)}, s ^ {(k - 2)}, \mu^ {(k - 1)}\right)\right) \\ \cdot \left(\| \ln x ^ {(k - 1)} - \ln x ^ {(k - 2)} \| _ {\tau (x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)})} ^ {2} + \| \ln s ^ {(k - 1)} - \ln s ^ {(k - 2)} \| _ {\tau (x ^ {(k - 1)}, s ^ {(k - 1)})} ^ {2}\right) \\ + o \left(\epsilon_ {h} ^ {2} \epsilon_ {b} d \log^ {2} n\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

$$
\operatorname {e r r} _ {\mathrm {i f c}} ^ {2} = o \left(\phi_ {b} \left(x ^ {(k - 1)}, x ^ {(k - 2)}, s ^ {(k - 2)}, \mu^ {(k - 2)}\right)\right)
$$

by lemma 53 and lemma 56. these can be bounded as follows

$$
\operatorname {e r r} _ {\mathrm {m i n}} ^ {2} \leq o \left(\zeta \epsilon^ {2} / \log^ {6} n\right) \cdot \frac {\epsilon}{2} + o \left(\zeta \epsilon^ {2} / \log^ {6} n\right)
$$

$$
\operatorname {e r r} _ {\mathrm {i f c}} ^ {2} \leq o \left(\zeta \epsilon^ {2} / \log^ {6} n\right)
$$

so for small enough  $\zeta > 0$  we get

$$
\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} e _ {x} \| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \operatorname {e r r} _ {\mathrm {m i n}} + \operatorname {e r r} _ {\mathrm {i f c}} \leq \frac {\gamma \alpha}{2 ^ {2 0}}
$$

where we use  $\gamma \alpha = \omega (\epsilon /\log^3 n)$

proof of theorem 5. note that, when ignoring feasibility, theorem 5 was proven as theorem 32. so we are only left with showing that  $\phi_b$  stays small and that the condition of theorem 32 is true, i.e. that the extra movement  $e_x$  of  $x$  by calling maintainfeasibility in algorithm 1 satisfies

$$
\left\| \mathbf {x} ^ {- 1} e _ {x} \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} \leq \frac {\gamma \alpha}{2 ^ {2 0}}.
$$

on one hand, the latter claim was proven in lemma 58, assuming the infeasibility potential  $\phi_b$  is small. on the other hand, lemma 54 shows that  $\phi_b$  does not change more than a multiplicative factor within  $\sqrt{d} / \log^6 n$  iterations as long as  $x$  and  $s$  do not change to much in each iteration. thus by induction we have that  $\phi_b$  is small and that  $x$  and  $s$  do not change much. after  $\sqrt{d} / \log^6 n$  iterations the potential is decreased again by lemma 55, so  $\phi_b(x, x, s, \mu)$  stays small even after  $\sqrt{d} \log^6 n$  iterations.

note that the claim, that  $\phi_b$  stays small throughout a sequence of  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}} (\sqrt{d})$  iterations, hold only with probability 0.5 by lemma 54. thus after every  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}} (\sqrt{d})$  iterations we may have to revert the changes of the past  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}} (\sqrt{d})$  iterations and retry. this increases the total runtime by another  $o(\log n)$  factor.

at last, note that during the last iteration of the ipm, we can apply lemma 55 once more to reduce

$$
\phi_ {b} (x, x, s, \mu) \leq \frac {\zeta \epsilon^ {2}}{\sqrt {d} \log^ {6} n}.
$$

we are left with analyzing the complexity of algorithm 11.

proof of theorem 7. we choose two accuracy parameters as follows:

$$
\epsilon_ {b} = \omega \left(d ^ {- 1 / 2} \epsilon^ {- 2} \log^ {- 2} n\right) \text {a n d} \epsilon_ {h} = \omega \left(d ^ {- 1 / 4} \epsilon^ {- 1} \log^ {- 3} n\right)
$$

then lemma 51 shows that computing  $\delta_{b}$  in maintaininfeasibility takes  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(d^{5/2}\epsilon^{-2})$  time. the products with  $\mathbf{h}_1, \mathbf{h}_2, \mathbf{h}_3, \mathbf{h}_4$  either need  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(d^2/\epsilon_h^2) = \widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(d^{5/2}\epsilon^{-2})$  time, or (in case of inverse matrices) require the same time as solving the systems in lines 12 and 13 of algorithm 1. so these

costs are subsumed and we do not have to count them. the algorithm used in lemma 55 involves computing  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}x - b$  which takes  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(nd)$  time and solving a linear system, which is subsumed by lines 12 and 13 of algorithm 1. since this happens every  $\widetilde{\omega}(\sqrt{d})$  iterations, the amortized cost is  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(n\sqrt{d})$ .

thus the total additional amortized cost is

$$
\widetilde {\mathrm {o}} (n \sqrt {d} + d ^ {5 / 2} \epsilon^ {- 2}).
$$

# c constructing the initial point

here we want to prove lemma 12 which is used to quickly find an initial point for our ipm. lemma 12 is an extension of the following known reduction. we modify this reduction so that we no longer require our final solution to be feasible.

lemma 59 ([ytm94, cls19]). consider a linear program  $\min_{\mathbf{a}^{\top}x = b,x\geq 0}c^{\top}x$  with  $n$  variables and  $d$  constraints. assume that

1. diameter of the polytope: for any  $x \geq 0$  with  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}x = b$ , we have that  $\| x \|_2 \leq r$ .  
2. lipschitz constant of the linear program:  $\| c\| _2\leq l$

for any  $\delta \in (0,1]$ , the modified linear program  $\min_{\overline{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\overline{x} = \overline{b},\overline{x}\geq 0}\overline{c}^{\top}\overline{x}$  with

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {a} & 1 _ {n} \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \\ 0 & 1 \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \\ \frac {1}{r} b ^ {\top} - 1 _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} & 0 \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {(n + 2) \times (d + 1)}, \overline {{b}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \frac {1}{r} b \\ (n + 1) \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {d + 1} a n d, \overline {{c}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \frac {\delta}{l} \cdot c \\ 0 \\ 1 \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n + 2}
$$

satisfies the following:

1.  $\overline{x} = \begin{bmatrix} 1_n \\ 1 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix}$ ,  $\overline{y} = \begin{bmatrix} 0_d \\ -1 \end{bmatrix}$  and  $\overline{s} = \begin{bmatrix} 1_n + \frac{\delta}{l} \cdot c \\ 1 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix}$  are feasible primal dual vectors.

2. for any feasible primal dual vectors  $(\overline{x},\overline{y},\overline{s})$  with duality gap  $\leq \delta^2$ , consider the vector  $\widehat{x} = r\cdot \overline{x}_{1:n}$ $(\overline{x}_{1:n}$  is the first  $n$  coordinates of  $\overline{x}\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$ ) is an approximate solution to the original linear program in the following sense

$$
c ^ {\top} \hat {x} \leq \min  _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x = b, x \geq 0} c ^ {\top} x + l r \cdot \delta ,
$$

$$
\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b \| _ {2} \leq 4 n ^ {2} \delta \cdot \left(\| a \| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}\right),
$$

$$
\widehat {x} \geq 0.
$$

theorem 12 (initial point). consider linear program  $\min_{\mathbf{a}^{\top}x = b,x\geq 0}c^{\top}x$  with  $n$  variables and  $d$  constraints. assume that

1. diameter of the polytope: for any  $x \geq 0$  with  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}x = b$ , we have that  $\| x \|_2 \leq r$ .  
2. lipschitz constant of the linear program:  $\| c\| _2\leq l$  
3. the constraint matrix  $\mathbf{a}$  is non-degenerate.

for any  $\delta \in (0,1]$ , the modified linear program  $\min_{\overline{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}\overline{x} = \overline{b},\overline{x}\geq 0}\overline{c}^{\top}\overline{x}$  with

$$
\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c c} \mathbf {a} & 1 _ {n} \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \\ 0 & 1 \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \\ \frac {1}{r} b ^ {\top} - 1 _ {n} ^ {\top} \mathbf {a} & 0 \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {(n + 2) \times (d + 1)},
$$

$$
\overline {{b}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \frac {1}{r} b \\ (n + 1) \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {d + 1} a n d, \overline {{c}} = \left[ \begin{array}{c} \frac {\delta}{l} \cdot c \\ 0 \\ 1 \end{array} \right] \in \mathbb {r} ^ {n + 2}
$$

satisfies the following:

1.  $\overline{x} = \begin{bmatrix} 1_n \\ 1 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix}$ ,  $\overline{y} = \begin{bmatrix} 0_d \\ -1 \end{bmatrix}$  and  $\overline{s} = \begin{bmatrix} 1_n + \frac{\delta}{l} \cdot c \\ 1 \\ 1 \end{bmatrix}$  are feasible primal dual vectors.

2. let  $(\overline{x},\overline{y},\overline{s})$  be primal dual vectors of the modified  $lp$  and  $\phi_b \stackrel{\mathrm{def}}{=} \frac{1}{\mu} \cdot \| \overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top \overline{x} - \overline{b} \|^2_{(\overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}} \mathbf{s}^{-1} \overline{\mathbf{a}})^{-1}}$ , then

$$
\| \overline {{x}} \| _ {\infty} \leq (1 + o (\phi_ {b})) \cdot o (n).
$$

3. let  $(\overline{x},\overline{y},\overline{s})$  be primal dual vectors of the modified lp with  $\overline{x}\cdot \overline{s}\approx_{0.5}\mu \cdot \tau (\overline{x},\overline{s})$  for  $\mu <  \delta^2 /(8d)$  and small enough  $\phi_b\coloneqq \frac{1}{\mu}\cdot \| \overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top \overline{x} -\overline{b}\|_{(\overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{a}})^{-1}}^2 = o(1)$  (i.e.  $\overline{x}$  does not have to be feasible). the vector  $\widehat{x}\stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}r\cdot \overline{x}_{1:n}$  where  $\overline{x}_{1:n}$  is the first  $n$  coordinates of  $\overline{x}$  is an approximate solution to the original linear program in the following sense

$$
c ^ {\top} \widehat {x} \leq \min  _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x = b, x \geq 0} c ^ {\top} x + o (n l r) \cdot (\sqrt {\phi_ {b}} + \delta),
$$

$$
\left. \left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b \right\| _ {2} \leq o (n ^ {2}) \cdot \left(\left\| \mathbf {a} \right\| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}\right) \cdot \left(\sqrt {\phi_ {b}} + \delta\right)\right),
$$

$$
\widehat {x} \geq 0.
$$

proof. assume for simplicity that the original input lp satisfies  $\| \mathbf{a}^\top 1_n - b / r\|_\infty \geq 0.5(\| \mathbf{a}\| _f + \| b\| /r)$ . if this assumption is not satisfied, then we can simply add a variable  $x_{n + 1}$  and the constraint  $(\| \mathbf{a}\| _f + \| b\| /r)x_{n + 1} = 0$ . note that this does not increase the value of  $\| b\| _2,\| c\| _2$  or  $r$  for this new lp. further, the constraint matrix,  $\mathbf{a}'\in \mathbb{r}^{(d + 1)\times (n + 1)}$  of this new lp satisfies  $\| \mathbf{a}'\| _f\leq 2\| \mathbf{a}\| _f + \| b\| /r$  and the new constraint vector  $b^{\prime}$  satisfies

$$
\left\| \left(\mathbf {a} ^ {\prime}\right) ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} - b ^ {\prime} / r \right\| _ {\infty} \geq \left(\left\| \mathbf {a} \right\| _ {f} + \left\| b \right\| _ {2} / r\right) \geq \frac {1}{2} \left(\left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\prime} \right\| _ {f} + \left\| b \right\| _ {2} / r\right)
$$

as desired. consequently, if we take this new lp as input to our reduction we only need to decrease  $\delta$  by a factor of 2 more than before to obtain the same result. so for now assume  $\| \mathbf{a}^\top 1_n - b\|_\infty \geq 0.5(\| \mathbf{a}\| _f + \| b\| /r)$ .

given an (infeasible) solution  $(\overline{x},\overline{y},\overline{s})\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}\times \mathbb{r}^{d + 1}\times \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$  to the modified lp, let  $(\overline{x}^{\prime},\overline{y},\overline{s})$  be the feasible solution we get via projecting

$$
\overline {{x}} ^ {\prime} = \overline {{x}} + \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}) ^ {- 1} (b - \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{x}}).
$$

then this new point is feasible because

$$
\begin{array}{l} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} x ^ {\prime} = \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \bar {x} + \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} (\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}) ^ {- 1} (b - \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \bar {x}) \\ = \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \bar {x} + (b - \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \bar {x}) = b, \\ \end{array}
$$

and by lemma 56 we have

$$
\left\| \overline {{\mathbf {x}}} ^ {- 1} \left(\bar {x} ^ {\prime} - \bar {x}\right) \right\| _ {\tau + \infty} ^ {2} = o \left(\phi_ {b}\right). \tag {c.1}
$$

as  $\overline{x}^{\prime}$  is feasible, we have  $\sum_{i\in [n + 1]}\overline{x}_i'\leq n + 1$  by the constraints of the modified lp and thus  $\overline{x}_i^\prime \leq n + 1$  for  $i\leq n + 1$ . to bound  $\theta \stackrel {\mathrm{def}}{=}\overline{x}_{n + 2}^{\prime}$  note that for all  $i$  with  $(\mathbf{a}^{\top}1_{n} - b / r)_{i}\neq 0$  we have by the constraint for the lp

$$
\theta = \frac {(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{x}} _ {1 : n} ^ {\prime} - b / r) _ {i}}{(\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} - b / r) _ {i}} = \frac {\left| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{x}} _ {1 : n} ^ {\prime} - b / r) _ {i} \right|}{\left| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} - b / r) _ {i} \right|}.
$$

by choosing  $i$  to be the maximizer of the absolute value of the denominator we have

$$
\theta \leq \frac {\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \overline {{x}} _ {1 : n} ^ {\prime} - b / r \| _ {\infty}}{\| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} - b / r) _ {i} \| _ {\infty}} \leq \frac {(\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} + \| b \| _ {2} / r) (n + 1)}{\| (\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} - b / r) _ {i} \| _ {\infty}} = o (n)
$$

where in the second step we used that  $\| \overline{x}_{1:n}^{\prime}\|_{2}\leq \sum_{j\in [n]}\overline{x}_{j}^{\prime}\leq n + 1$  and in the third step we used the assumption that  $\| \mathbf{a}^\top 1_n - b / r\|_\infty \geq 0.5(\| \mathbf{a}\| _f + \| b\| _2 / r)$ . thus in summary we have  $\| \overline{x}^{\prime}\|_{\infty} = o(n)$

and  $\| \overline{x} \|_{\infty} \leq (1 + o(\phi_b)) \cdot o(n)$  by (c.1). this concludes the proof on  $\| \overline{x} \|_{\infty}$  and we are left with proving the last claim of theorem 12.

for our feasible solution  $(\overline{x}^{\prime},\overline{y},\overline{s})$  we can bound the duality gap as follows

$$
\begin{array}{l} \sum_ {i \in [ n + 2 ]} \overline {{x}} _ {i} ^ {\prime} \cdot \overline {{s}} _ {i} = \sum_ {i \in [ n + 2 ]} \overline {{x}} _ {i} \cdot \overline {{s}} _ {i} (1 + \overline {{x}} _ {i} ^ {- 1} (\overline {{x}} _ {i} ^ {\prime} - \overline {{x}} _ {i})) \\ \leq \sum_ {i \in [ n + 2 ]} 2 \mu \tau_ {i} (1 + o (\sqrt {\phi_ {b}})) \\ = 4 \mu d \left(1 + o \left(\sqrt {\phi_ {b}}\right)\right) \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used  $\overline{x}_i\cdot \overline{s}_i\leq 2\mu \tau_i$ $\tau_{i} = \sigma_{i} + n / d$ $\sum_{i}\sigma_{i} = d$  and the previous bound on  $\| \overline{\mathbf{x}}^{-1}(\overline{x}^{\prime} - \overline{x})\|_{\tau +\infty}^{2}$  so for  $\mu \leq \delta^2 /(8d)$  and small enough  $\phi_b = o(1)$ , this duality gap is bounded by  $\delta^2$ . this allows us to apply lemma 59, so we can construct a good solution  $\hat{x}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{r}^{n}$  for the original lp from  $\overline{x}^{\prime}\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$  by taking the first  $n$  coordinates and scaling them by  $r$ . we further construct an  $\hat{x}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  from our infeasible solution  $\overline{x}\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$  in the same way. for this infeasible  $\hat{x}\in \mathbb{r}^n$  we then have

$$
\begin{array}{l} c ^ {\top} \hat {x} = c ^ {\top} \hat {x} ^ {\prime} + c ^ {\top} (\hat {x} - \hat {x} ^ {\prime}) \\ \leq \min _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x = b, x \geq 0} c ^ {\top} x + l r \cdot \delta + c ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}). \\ \end{array}
$$

the impact of the last error term can be bounded by

$$
\begin{array}{l} c ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \leq \| c ^ {\top} \| _ {2} \cdot \| \widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2} \leq l \| \widehat {x} ^ {\prime} \cdot \widehat {x} ^ {\prime - 1} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \\ \leq l \| \widehat {x} ^ {\prime} \| _ {2} \cdot \| \widehat {x} ^ {\prime - 1} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {\infty} \\ \leq l r \cdot o \left(n \sqrt {\phi_ {b}}\right), \\ \end{array}
$$

where we used that  $\overline{x} \in \mathbb{r}^{n+2}$  is feasible, so  $\sum_{i \in [n+1]} \overline{x}_i' = n + 1$ , which means  $\| \widehat{x}' \|_2 \leq \| \widehat{x}' \|_1 \leq r(n + 1)$ . the last step uses that the entries of  $\overline{x}' \in \mathbb{r}^{n+2}$  and  $\overline{x} \in \mathbb{r}^{n+2}$  are close multiplicatively, so the same is also true for  $\widehat{x} \in \mathbb{r}^n$  and  $\widehat{x}' \in \mathbb{r}^n$ . in summary we thus obtain

$$
c ^ {\top} \hat {x} \leq \min  _ {\mathbf {a} ^ {\top} x = b, x \geq 0} c ^ {\top} x + l r \cdot (\delta + o (n \sqrt {\phi_ {b}})).
$$

we are left with proving the bound on  $\| \mathbf{a}^\top \widehat{x} - b \|_2$ . for this note that

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b \| _ {2} \leq \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} ^ {\prime} - b \| _ {2} + \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \\ \leq 4 n ^ {2} \delta \cdot \left(\| a \| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}\right) + \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\hat {x} - \hat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \\ \end{array}
$$

via triangle inequality and lemma 59. so it remains to bound  $\| \mathbf{a}^{\top}(\widehat{x} -\widehat{x}^{\prime})\|_{2}$ . let  $x\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 1}$  be the first  $n + 1$  entries of  $\overline{x}\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$  and  $\theta \in \mathbb{r}$  be the last entry of  $\overline{x}\in \mathbb{r}^{n + 2}$  (and likewise  $x^{\prime}$  and  $\theta^{\prime}$  for the feasible  $\overline{x}^{\prime}$ ), then

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} (\overline {{x}} - \overline {{x}} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} = \frac {1}{r ^ {2}} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) + (b - r \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n}) (\theta - \theta^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} + (1 _ {n + 1} ^ {\top} (x - x ^ {\prime})) ^ {2} \\ \geq \frac {1}{r ^ {2}} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) + (b - r \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n}) (\theta - \theta^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} ^ {2}. \\ \end{array}
$$

hence, we have

$$
\left\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \left(\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {2} \leq \left\| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {x} - \bar {x} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {2} r + \left\| b - r \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} \right\| _ {2} \left| \theta - \theta^ {\prime} \right|. \tag {c.2}
$$

we first bound the term  $\| \overline{\mathbf{a}}^{\top}(\overline{x} -\overline{x}^{\prime})\|_{2}$  in (c.2). note that  $\phi_b = \mu^{-1}\| \overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top (\overline{x} -\overline{x}^{\prime})\|_{(\overline{\mathbf{a}}^\top \overline{\mathbf{x}}\mathbf{s}^{-1}\overline{\mathbf{a}})^{-1}}^2$  so we bound the  $\ell_2$ -norm via this local norm.

$$
\begin{array}{l} \left\| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} (\bar {x} - \bar {x} ^ {\prime}) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq \frac {1}{\lambda_ {\min } ((\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}) ^ {- 1})} \left\| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} (\bar {x} - \bar {x} ^ {\prime}) \right\| _ {(\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}) ^ {- 1}} ^ {2} \\ = \lambda_ {\max } \left(\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {x s}}} ^ {- 1} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}\right) \mu \phi_ {b} \\ \leq \left(\max  _ {i \in [ n ]} \bar {x} _ {i} / \bar {s} _ {i}\right) \lambda_ {\max } \left(\overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \overline {{\mathbf {a}}}\right) \mu \phi_ {b} \\ \leq \left(\max  _ {i \in [ n ]} \overline {{x}} _ {i} / \overline {{s}} _ {i}\right) \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \mu \phi_ {b} \\ \leq o (1) \cdot \left(\max  _ {i \in [ n ]} x ^ {\prime} _ {i} / \bar {s} _ {i}\right) \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \mu \phi_ {b} \\ \end{array}
$$

where in the last step we used that  $x$  and  $x'$  differ by an  $1 \pm o(\sqrt{\phi_b}) = o(1)$  factor. we already argued  $\| x' \|_{\infty} = o(n)$  and we have  $x's \geq 0.5\tau \mu$ , so

$$
1 / s _ {i} \leq 2 x _ {i} ^ {\prime} / (\tau \mu) \leq 2 n x _ {i} ^ {\prime} / (d \mu).
$$

with the previous bounds on  $x$  this leads to

$$
\max _ {i \in [ n ]} x _ {i} ^ {\prime} / s _ {i} = o (n ^ {3} / (d \mu)) = o (n ^ {3} / (d \mu)),
$$

which means

$$
\left\| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} ^ {\top} \left(\bar {x} - \bar {x} ^ {\prime}\right) \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq o \left(\left(n ^ {3} / d\right) \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \phi_ {b}\right). \tag {c.3}
$$

now, we bound the term  $\| b - \mathbf{a}^\top 1_n\|_2|\theta - \theta'|$  in (c.2). since we know that  $|\theta|$  and  $|\theta'|$  are bounded by  $o(n\delta)$  (this is proven in [cls19] where they proved lemma 59), we have the bound

$$
\left\| b - r \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} \right\| _ {2} ^ {2} \cdot | \theta - \theta^ {\prime} | ^ {2} \leq o (n \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} ^ {2} r ^ {2} + \| b \| _ {2} ^ {2}) \cdot n ^ {2} \delta^ {2}. \tag {c.4}
$$

putting (c.3) and (c.4) into (c.2), we have

$$
\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} ^ {2} \leq o ((n ^ {3} / d) \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} \| _ {f} ^ {2} \phi_ {b} r ^ {2}) + o (n \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} ^ {2} r ^ {2} + \| b \| _ {2} ^ {2}) \cdot n ^ {2} \delta^ {2}.
$$

at last we can bound

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \overline {{\mathbf {a}}} \| _ {f} \leq \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} + (n + 1) ^ {0. 5} \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} + \| \frac {1}{r} b - \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} 1 _ {n} \| _ {2} \\ \leq o (n ^ {0. 5} \| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} + \frac {1}{r} \| b \| _ {2}), \\ \end{array}
$$

so, in total, we obtain

$$
\| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \leq o \left(\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} r n ^ {2} + \| b \| _ {2} n\right) \cdot \left(\sqrt {\phi_ {b}} + \delta\right).
$$

and hence

$$
\begin{array}{l} \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} - b \| _ {2} \leq \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} \widehat {x} ^ {\prime} - b \| _ {2} + \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\widehat {x} - \widehat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \\ \leq 4 n ^ {2} \delta \cdot \left(\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}\right) + \| \mathbf {a} ^ {\top} (\hat {x} - \hat {x} ^ {\prime}) \| _ {2} \\ \leq 4 n ^ {2} \delta \cdot \left(\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}\right) + o \left(\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} r n ^ {2} + \| b \| _ {2} n\right) \cdot \left(\sqrt {\phi_ {b}} + \delta\right) \\ \leq o (n ^ {2}) \cdot (\| \mathbf {a} \| _ {f} r + \| b \| _ {2}) \cdot (\sqrt {\phi_ {b}} + \delta). \\ \end{array}
$$

algorithm 12: algorithm for maintaining  $\nabla \phi (\overline{v})^{\flat}$  and  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\nabla \phi (\overline{v})^{\flat}$  (theorem 60)  
1 members   
2  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)}\in \{0,1\} ^n$  // indicator vectors for a partition of [n]   
3  $w^{(k,\ell)}\in \mathbb{r}^d$  // maintained to be  $\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$    
4  $x,v,\overline{\tau}\in \mathbb{r}^n$    
5 procedure initialize(a  $\in \mathbb{r}^{n\times d}$ $v\in \mathbb{r}^n$ $\tau \in \mathbb{r}^n$ $x\in \mathbb{r}^n$ $\epsilon >0$    
6 a  $\leftarrow$  a,  $x\gets x$ $v\gets v$    
7 for  $k = 1,\ldots ,\log^{-1}(1 - \epsilon)$  and  $\ell = 0,\dots,(1.5 / \epsilon)$  do   
8  $\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}\gets 0_{n}$    
9  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)}\gets 1$  and  $\overline{\tau}_i\gets (1 - \epsilon)^{k + 1}$  if  $0.5 + \ell \epsilon /2\leq v_i <   0.5 + (\ell +1)\epsilon /2$  and   
10  $(1 - \epsilon)^{k + 1}\leq \tau_{i}\leq (1 - \epsilon)^{k}.$    
11 procedure update(i  $\in [n]$ $a\in \mathbb{r},b\in \mathbb{r},c\in \mathbb{r})$    
12 find  $k,\ell$  such that  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)} = 1$  , then  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)}\gets 0$    
13  $w^{(k,\ell)}\gets w^{(k,\ell)} - \mathbf{a}^\top x_i1_i$    
14 find  $k,\ell$  such that  $0.5 + \ell \epsilon /2\leq a <   0.5 + (\ell +1)\epsilon /2$  and  $(1 - \epsilon)^{k + 1}\leq b\leq (1 - \epsilon)^{k}$  , then   
15  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)}\gets 0.$    
16  $w^{(k,\ell)}\gets w^{(k,\ell)} + \mathbf{a}^\top c1_i$    
17 procedure query()   
18 find  $s^{(k,l)}$  with argmax_||w|2+||w/√τ||∞≤1<|∇φ(v)/√τ,w>∑k,l s(k,l)ov(k,ℓ) via algorithm   
8 from [ls19].   
19 return ∑k,l s(k,l)ov(k,ℓ) and ∑k,l s(k,l) a t xov(k,ℓ)

# d gradient maintenance

in this section we provide a data structure for efficiently maintaining  $\nabla \phi (\overline{v})^{\flat}$  and  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\nabla \phi (\overline{v})^{\flat}$  in our ipm, i.e. algorithm 1.

theorem 60. there exists a deterministic data-structure that supports the following operations

- initializize ( $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}, v \in \mathbb{r}^n, \tau \in \mathbb{r}^n, x \in \mathbb{r}^n, \epsilon > 0$ ): the data-structure preprocesses the given matrix  $\mathbf{a} \in \mathbb{r}^{n \times d}$ , vectors  $v, \tau, x \in \mathbb{r}^n$ , and accuracy parameter  $\epsilon > 0$  in  $o(nd)$  time. the data-structure assumes  $0.5 \leq v \leq 2$  and  $d/n \leq \tau \leq 2$ .  
-  $\text{update}(i, a, b, c)$ : sets  $v_i = a$ ,  $\tau_i = b$  and  $x_i = c$  in  $o(d)$  time. the data-structure assumes  $0.5 \leq v \leq 2$  and  $d / n \leq \tau \leq 2$ .  
- query(): returns  $\nabla \phi(\overline{v})^{\flat}$  and  $\mathbf{a}^{\top} \mathbf{x} \nabla \phi(\overline{v})^{\flat}$  for  $\|\overline{v}-v\|_{\infty} \leq \epsilon$  in  $\widetilde{\mathrm{o}}(n)$  time. see lemma 28 for  $(\cdot)^{\flat}$ .

proof. we start by explaining the algorithm and analyzing its complexity. afterwards we prove the correctness.

algorithm: during preprocessing and updates, the algorithm maintains a partition  $v^{(k,\ell)} \subset [n]$  where  $i \in v^{(k,\ell)}$  if

$$
0. 5 + \ell \epsilon / 2 \leq v _ {i} <   0. 5 + (\ell + 1) \epsilon / 2
$$

$$
(1 - \epsilon) ^ {k + 1} \leq \tau_ {i} \leq (1 - \epsilon) ^ {k}.
$$

additionally the data-structure maintains the products  $\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$  where  $\overline{v}_i^{(k,\ell)} = 1$  if  $i\in v^{(k,\ell)}$  and  $v_{i}^{(k,\ell)} = 0$  otherwise.

during queries the data-structure computes the following: define  $\phi(x) \coloneqq \lambda(\exp(\lambda(x - 1)) - \exp(-\lambda(x - 1))) = (\nabla \phi(x))_i$ , then we apply algorithm 8 from [ls19] to compute

$$
\sum_ {k, \ell} s ^ {(k, \ell)} \bar {v} ^ {(k, \ell)} = \operatorname * {a r g m a x} _ {\| w \| _ {2} + \| w / \sqrt {\bar {\tau}} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \left\langle \sum_ {k, \ell} \phi (0. 5 + \ell \epsilon / 2) \cdot \bar {v} ^ {(k, \ell)} / \sqrt {\bar {\tau}}, w \right\rangle
$$

in  $o(n\log n)$  time and then return  $\sum_{k,\ell}s^{(k,\ell)}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$  and  $\sum_{k,\ell}s^{(k,\ell)}\mathbf{a}^{\top}\mathbf{x}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$ . the solution can be represented by  $\sum_{k,\ell}s^{(k,\ell)}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$  because all coordinates with the same value of  $\tau_{i}$  and  $x_{i}$  has the same  $w_{i}$  in the solution. this can be seen easily from the description of algorithm 8 from [ls19].

**complexity:** every entry changes to  $v, \tau$  might move an index  $i$  from some  $v^{(k,\ell)}$  to another  $v^{(k,\ell)}$  which corresponds to changing one entry of  $\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$  and  $\overline{v}^{(k',\ell')}$ . so every update to  $v, \tau, x$  costs only  $o(d)$  time to update  $\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x}\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}$ .

correctness: for the given vector  $v$ , we can split  $v$  into groups by grouping the entries to multiples of  $\epsilon / 2$ . by assumption we have  $0.5 \leq v \leq 2$ , so we have at most  $o(1 / \epsilon)$  many groups. by rounding down  $v$  on each of these groups we obtain

$$
\bar {v} := \sum_ {k} (0. 5 + k \epsilon / 2) \cdot \sum_ {i: 0. 5 + k \epsilon / 2 \leq v _ {i} <   0. 5 + (k + 1) \epsilon / 2} 1 _ {i}
$$

which satisfies  $\| \overline{v} - v \|_{\infty} \leq \epsilon / 2$ . for notational simplicity define

$$
\overline {{v}} ^ {(k)} := \sum_ {i: 0. 5 + k \epsilon / 2 \leq v _ {i} <   0. 5 + (k + 1) \epsilon / 2} 1 _ {i}
$$

then  $\overline{v} = (0.5 + k\epsilon /2)\sum_{k}\overline{v}^{(k)}$  and for  $\phi (x)\coloneqq \lambda (\exp (\lambda (x - 1)) - \exp (-\lambda (x - 1))) = (\nabla \phi (x))_i$  we have

$$
\begin{array}{l} \nabla \phi (\bar {v}) = \sum_ {k} \nabla \phi ((0. 5 + k \epsilon / 2) \cdot \bar {v} ^ {(k)}) \\ = \sum_ {k} \phi (0. 5 + k \epsilon / 2) \cdot \bar {v} ^ {(k)}. \\ \end{array}
$$

next we split  $[n]$  into  $o(\epsilon^{-1}\log (n / d))$  many groups based on multiplicative approximations of  $\tau$ , in other words we have that

$$
\overline {{\tau}} := \sum_ {k} (1 - \epsilon) ^ {k} \sum_ {i: (1 - \epsilon) ^ {k + 1} \leq \tau_ {i} <   (1 - \epsilon) ^ {k}} 1 _ {i}
$$

is an entry-wise  $(1\pm \epsilon)$ -approximation of  $\tau$ . then for  $\overline{v}^{(k,\ell)}\coloneqq \overline{v}^{(k)}\cdot \sum_{i:(1 - \epsilon)^{k + 1}\leq \tau_i < (1 - \epsilon)^k}1_i$ , we have

$$
\nabla \phi (\bar {v}) / \sqrt {\bar {\tau}} = \sum_ {k, \ell} (1 - \epsilon) ^ {k} \cdot \phi (0. 5 + \ell \epsilon / 2) \cdot \bar {v} ^ {(k, \ell)}.
$$

next, algorithm 8 from [ls19] shows how to compute scalars  $s^{(k,l)}$  in  $o(n\log n)$  time with

$$
\operatorname *{argmax}_{\| w\|_{2} + \| w / \sqrt{\overline{\tau}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\langle \nabla \phi (\bar{v}) / \sqrt{\overline{\tau}},w\right\rangle = \sum_{k,l}s^{(k,l)}_{\overline{v}}^{(k,\ell)}.
$$

note that there is  $\| \overline{v}^{\prime} - \overline{v}\|_{\infty}\leq \epsilon /\lambda$  with

$$
\begin{array}{l} \operatorname *{argmax}_{\| w\|_{2} + \| w / \sqrt{\overline{\tau}}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\langle \nabla \phi (\bar{v}) / \sqrt{\overline{\tau}},w\right\rangle \\ = \operatorname * {a r g m a x} _ {\| w \sqrt {\overline {{\tau}} / \tau} \| _ {2} + \| w / \sqrt {\tau} \| _ {\infty} \leq \sqrt {\overline {{\tau}} / \tau}} \left\langle \nabla \phi (\overline {{v}}) / \sqrt {\overline {{\tau}}}, w \right\rangle \\ = \operatorname * {a r g m a x} _ {\| w \sqrt {\overline {{\tau}} / \tau} \| _ {2} + \| w / \sqrt {\tau} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \left\langle \nabla \phi (\bar {v}) / \sqrt {\bar {\tau}}, w \cdot \sqrt {\bar {\tau} / \tau} \right\rangle \\ = \operatorname *{argmax}_{\| w\sqrt{\overline{\tau} / \tau}\|_{2} + \| w / \sqrt{\tau}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}}\left\langle \nabla \phi (\bar{v}) / \sqrt{\tau},w\right\rangle \\ = (1\pm \epsilon)\operatorname *{argmax}_{\| w\|_{2} + \| w / \sqrt{\tau}\|_{\infty}\leq 1}\left\langle \nabla \phi (\overline{v}) / \sqrt{\tau},w\right\rangle \\ = \operatorname * {a r g m a x} _ {\| w \| _ {2} + \| w / \sqrt {\tau} \| _ {\infty} \leq 1} \left\langle \nabla \phi (\overline {{v}} ^ {\prime}) / \sqrt {\tau}, w \right\rangle \\ = \nabla \phi (\bar {v} ^ {\prime}) ^ {\flat} \\ \end{array}
$$

so  $\sum_{k,l} s^{(k,l)} \overline{v}^{(k,\ell)} = \nabla \phi(\overline{v}')^{\flat}$  for some  $\| \overline{v}' - v \|_{\infty} \leq \epsilon$ . thus the algorithm returns  $\nabla \phi(\overline{v}')^{\flat}$  and  $\mathbf{a}^\top \mathbf{x} \nabla \phi(\overline{v}')^{\flat}$ .
